Run systematic **technical** quality checks and generate a comprehensive report. Don't fix issues — document them for other commands to address.

This is a code-level audit, not a design critique. Check what's measurable and verifiable in the implementation.

## Diagnostic Scan

Run comprehensive checks across 5 dimensions. Score each dimension 0-4 using the criteria below.

### 1. Accessibility (A11y)

**Check for**:
- **Contrast issues**: Text contrast ratios < 4.5:1 (or 7:1 for AAA)
- **Missing ARIA**: Interactive elements without proper roles, labels, or states
- **Keyboard navigation**: Missing focus indicators, illogical tab order, keyboard traps
- **Semantic HTML**: Improper heading hierarchy, missing landmarks, divs instead of buttons
- **Alt text**: Missing or poor image descriptions
- **Form issues**: Inputs without labels, poor error messaging, missing required indicators

**Score 0-4**: 0=Inaccessible (fails WCAG A), 1=Major gaps (few ARIA labels, no keyboard nav), 2=Partial (some a11y effort, significant gaps), 3=Good (WCAG AA mostly met, minor gaps), 4=Excellent (WCAG AA fully met, approaches AAA)

### 2. Performance

**Check for**:
- **Layout thrashing**: Reading/writing layout properties in loops
- **Expensive animations**: Casual layout-property animation, unbounded blur/filter/shadow effects, or effects that visibly drop frames
- **Missing optimization**: Images without lazy loading, unoptimized assets, missing will-change
- **Bundle size**: Unnecessary imports, unused dependencies
- **Render performance**: Unnecessary re-renders, missing memoization

**Score 0-4**: 0=Severe issues (layout thrash, unoptimized everything), 1=Major problems (no lazy loading, expensive animations), 2=Partial (some optimization, gaps remain), 3=Good (mostly optimized, minor improvements possible), 4=Excellent (fast, lean, well-optimized)

### 3. Theming

**Check for**:
- **Hard-coded colors**: Colors not using design tokens
- **Broken dark mode**: Missing dark mode variants, poor contrast in dark theme
- **Inconsistent tokens**: Using wrong tokens, mixing token types
- **Theme switching issues**: Values that don't update on theme change

**Score 0-4**: 0=No theming (hard-coded everything), 1=Minimal tokens (mostly hard-coded), 2=Partial (tokens exist but inconsistently used), 3=Good (tokens used, minor hard-coded values), 4=Excellent (full token system, dark mode works perfectly)

### 4. Responsive Design

**Check for**:
- **Fixed widths**: Hard-coded widths that break on mobile
- **Touch targets**: Interactive elements < 44x44px
- **Horizontal scroll**: Content overflow on narrow viewports
- **Text scaling**: Layouts that break when text size increases
- **Missing breakpoints**: No mobile/tablet variants

**Score 0-4**: 0=Desktop-only (breaks on mobile), 1=Major issues (some breakpoints, many failures), 2=Partial (works on mobile, rough edges), 3=Good (responsive, minor touch target or overflow issues), 4=Excellent (fluid, all viewports, proper touch targets)

### 5. Anti-Patterns (CRITICAL)

Check against ALL the **DON'T** guidelines from the parent impeccable skill (already loaded in this context). Look for AI slop tells (AI color palette, gradient text, glassmorphism, hero metrics, card grids, generic fonts) and general design anti-patterns (gray on color, nested cards, bounce easing, redundant copy).

**Score 0-4**: 0=AI slop gallery (5+ tells), 1=Heavy AI aesthetic (3-4 tells), 2=Some tells (1-2 noticeable), 3=Mostly clean (subtle issues only), 4=No AI tells (distinctive, intentional design)

# Product

<!-- impeccable:product-schema 1 -->

## Scope

App-wide only. Every statement in this file must be true of all of LSAT U.

A rule that binds one surface does not belong here. It goes in that surface's brief under `.impeccable/surfaces/`. This split is deliberate and is the correction to a real failure: the previous PRODUCT.md was written on 2026-05-02 for the Intro to LR bootcamp alone, then widened app-wide on 2026-05-08 in a commit whose stated purpose was adding messenger principles. Bootcamp anti-references silently became binding on the drills, and stayed binding for four months. A one-surface rule written into an app-wide file has no boundary that can hold it. Keeping the file app-wide only removes the mechanism.

The repo already carries eight bounded contexts with their own glossaries (`CONTEXT-MAP.md`). Surface-specific product truth follows those boundaries, not this file.

## Platform

web

## Users

**Primary: Aspiring Attorneys clients.** Students who are paying Joshua for one-to-one LSAT coaching. They have consultations, scheduled sessions, transcripts, assigned work, and a real back-and-forth with him. The platform is where their coaching lives between sessions. They arrive already knowing who their coach is, and design decisions are made for them first.

**Secondary: independent self-study learners.** Students using the platform without an active coaching relationship. They are served by the same surfaces but are not the audience a tradeoff is resolved in favour of.

**Operator: Joshua.** The sole instructor and the product, voice, and content authority. He assigns work, publishes material, reviews student activity, and answers messages. He works from any device at any hour, so operator surfaces are as responsive as student surfaces.

## Product Purpose

LSAT U is the operational platform for Joshua's coaching practice. It delivers the method, holds the student's work, and keeps the coaching relationship live between sessions.

For a student: one place that knows what they have done, what they got wrong, and what to do next.

For Joshua: one workspace to assign, publish, review, and reply without losing the one-to-one feel that his practice is sold on.

Success is a student who logs in without being chased, knows the next action without asking, and can point to what improved.

## Positioning

**A real coach is in it.**

The claim a competing product cannot truthfully copy is that a named human being is on the other side of the platform, has met this student, and has looked at this student's work. Assignments come from him. Messages are answered by him. The material was authored by him. Nothing in the product should be built in a way that would still be true if the coach were removed, and nothing should imply a coach is present where one is not.

## Operating Context

The platform sits inside a coaching practice that runs partly outside it. Future work must not assume the app is the whole system.

- Coaching sessions happen on video and are transcribed. Session records, notes, and recaps originate outside the app.
- Booking and payment run through their own flow (packages, booking passes, a public booking microsite, single sessions).
- Students also practice on third-party platforms. Work done elsewhere is real work, and the app is not the only record of a student's effort.
- Joshua authors material ahead of time and assigns it, rather than the system generating it on demand.
- Students use both desktop and mobile, in focused study blocks and in short check-ins.

## Capabilities and Constraints

Confirmed surfaces: authentication and onboarding, the foyer, the classroom, guided drills and the question bank, full-passage Reading Comprehension, the Activity Centre, the Testing Centre and practice test runs, the wrong answer journal, saved sets, analytics and history, scheduling and booking, the inbox, the bootcamps, and an admin and teach...
[/external_agent_tool_result]

/**
 * The Testing Centre.
 *
 * Two doors, and only two: sit one real section, or sit a full practice test.
 * Both are things you do under a clock with nothing revealed until the end,
 * which is what separates this room from Drill next door — drilling is practice
 * you stop and think inside, and a sitting is not. Keeping them apart is the
 * whole reason this is its own rail entry: nobody should start a 35:00 clock
 * while looking for a ten-question warm-up.
 *
 * Neither door owns a question runner. The section door hands off to the
 * launcher the drill hub already uses; the practice-test door hands off to the
 * same two runners, one leg at a time.
 */
import * as React from "react";
import { useNavigate } from "react-router-dom";
import { Loader2 } from "lucide-react";
import { Button } from "@/components/ui/button";
import {
  Select,
  SelectContent,
  SelectItem,
  SelectTrigger,
  SelectValue,
} from "@/components/ui/select";
import { useQuestionBank } from "@/contexts/QuestionBankContext";
import { SectionSelector } from "@/components/drill/SectionSelector";
import { loadAllIndexPassages } from "@/features/rc/lib/rcBank";
import { buildRcSections, type RcSection } from "@/features/rc/lib/rcSections";
import {
  buildPracticeTests,
  createPracticeTestRun,
  deletePracticeTestRun,
  formatPracticeTestClock,
  legLabel,
  listPracticeTestRuns,
  PRACTICE_TEST_PACES,
  type PracticeTestBlueprint,
  type PracticeTestPaceId,
  type PracticeTestRun,
} from "@/lib/practiceTest";
import { practiceTestPath } from "@/lib/practiceTestRunner";
import "./testing-centre.css";

type Door = null | "section" | "test";

/** RC sections load once and are shared by both doors. */
function useRcSections() {
  const [sections, setSections] = React.useState<RcSection[] | null>(null);
  const [error, setError] = React.useState<string | null>(null);

  React.useEffect(() => {
    let cancelled = false;
    loadAllIndexPassages()
      .then((passages) => {
        if (!cancelled) setSections(buildRcSections(passages));
      })
      .catch(() => {
        if (!cancelled) setError("Reading Comprehension sections could not be loaded.");
      });
    return () => {
      cancelled = true;
    };
  }, []);

  return { sections, error };
}

function PracticeTestSelector({

          No PrepTest in the bank currently has the two full Logical Reasoning
          sections and the full Reading Comprehension section a sitting needs.
          Single sections are still available through the other door.
        </p>
      </div>
    );
  }

  return (
    <div className="testing-panel">
      <div className="testing-field">
        <label htmlFor="testing-pt">PrepTest</label>
        <Select value={pt ? String(pt) : ""} onValueChange={(v) => setPt(Number(v))}>
          <SelectTrigger id="testing-pt">

          </SelectContent>

/**
 * The Testing Centre.
 *
 * Deliberately quieter than the drill hub: this is the room where a clock
 * starts, so it reads as a lobby with two doors rather than a launcher with
 * options. Colour is carried by the classroom tokens, so it follows light and
 * dark with everything else.
 */

.classroom-root .testing-lede {
  color: var(--classroom-meta);
  font-size: 15px;
  line-height: 1.6;
  margin: -12px 0 32px;
  max-width: 62ch;
}

.classroom-root .testing-doors {
  display: grid;
  gap: 16px;
  grid-template-columns: repeat(2, minmax(0, 1fr));
  margin-bottom: 40px;
}

@media (max-width: 900px) {
  .classroom-root .testing-doors {
    grid-template-columns: minmax(0, 1fr);
  }
}

.classroom-root .testing-door {
  background: var(--classroom-panel);
  border: 1px solid var(--classroom-line);
  border-radius: var(--classroom-radius);
  cursor: pointer;
  display: flex;
  flex-direction: column;
  gap: 8px;
  padding: 24px;
  text-align: left;
  transition:
    background-color var(--classroom-transition) ease,
    border-color var(--classroom-transition) ease,
    transform var(--classroom-transition) ease;
}

.classroom-root .testing-door:hover {
  background: var(--classroom-hover);
  transform: translateY(-1px);
}

.classroom-root .testing-door[aria-pressed='true'] {
  border-color: var(--classroom-ink);
}

.classroom-root .testing-door h2 {
  font-size: 18px;
  font-weight: 700;
  letter-spacing: -0.01em;
  margin: 0;
}

.classroom-root .testing-door p {
  color: var(--classroom-meta);
  font-size: 13.5px;
  line-height: 1.55;
  margin: 0;
}

.classroom-root .testing-door-meta {
  color: var(--classroom-meta-soft);
  font-size: 11px;
  font-weight: 600;
  letter-spacing: 0.08em;
  margin-top: 6px;
  text-transform: uppercase;
}

.classroom-root .testing-panel {
  background: var(--classroom-panel);
  border: 1px solid var(--classroom-line);
  border-radius: var(--classroom-radius);
  padding: 24px;
}

.classroom-root .testing-field {
  display: grid;
  gap: 6px;
  margin-bottom: 18px;
}

.classroom-root .testing-field > label {
  font-size: 12px;
  font-weight: 600;
  letter-spacing: 0.04em;
  text-transform: uppercase;
}

.classroom-root .testing-shape {
  border: 1px solid var(--classroom-line);
  border-radius: var(--classroom-radius);
  display: grid;
  gap: 0;
  margin: 20px 0;
  overflow: hidden;
}

.classroom-root .testing-shape-row {

.classroom-root .testing-shape-row + .testing-shape-row {

}

}

const LOCAL_DEV_HOSTS = new Set(["localhost", "127.0.0.1", "::1"]);

/**
 * Opt-in only. A plain `npm run dev` now exercises the real permission path, so
 * a gating regression is visible locally instead of surfacing after deploy.
 * Set VITE_DEV_BYPASS_GATES=1 in .env.local to walk gated routes without the
 * matching flags.
 *
 * It relaxes *flags*, never authentication — the caller applies it only after
 * the signed-in check, so no configuration can make this component render to an
 * anonymous visitor.
 */
function isLocalDevAccessAllowed() {
  return (
    import.meta.env.DEV &&
    import.meta.env.VITE_DEV_BYPASS_GATES === "1" &&
    LOCAL_DEV_HOSTS.has(window.location.hostname)
  );
}

export default function ProtectedRoute({ flag, requireAdmin, allowSignedIn, children }: ProtectedRouteProps) {
  const { user, authReady } = useAuth();
  const permissions = useUserPermissions();
  const location = useLocation();

  // Auth first, and nothing before it. `useUserPermissions` reports
  // `loading: authReady && !!user && isPending`, which is *false* while auth is
  // still resolving — so a check on `!user` alone read "not yet known" as
  // "signed out" and bounced every cold deep link to /auth. Holding here until
  // auth is definitively resolved is what makes an assignment link survive.
[/external_agent_tool_result]

=== classroom.css meta comment block ===
.classroom-root {
  --classroom-line: #f3e3d6;
  --classroom-ink: #43403c;
  --classroom-ink-body: #43403c;
  --classroom-meta: 166, 162, 158;
  --classroom-hover: rgba(246, 235, 225, 0.4);
  --classroom-dash: rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.15);
  --classroom-link: #ff8906;
  /* Text-safe pair. --classroom-link and --classroom-meta are tuned for fills,
     rules and decorative use, where WCAG 1.4.11's 3:1 does not apply. Against
     white they measure 2.38:1 and 2.54:1, so they fail 1.4.3 as TEXT.
     Use these two instead whenever the colour carries words or notation.
     Same hue (32deg / 30deg), darkened only in value: 5.39:1 and 5.38:1 on
     white, and >=4.5:1 on every 10% tint used in the classroom. */
  --classroom-link-text: #a25704;
  --classroom-meta-text: #6d6a67;
  --classroom-active: #43403c;
  --classroom-active-ink: #ffffff;
  --classroom-meta-soft: rgba(67, 64, 60, 0.4);
  --classroom-panel: #ffffff;
  --classroom-page: #ffffff;
  --classroom-drill-page: #fdf8f3;
  --classroom-trigger: #fefcf7;
  --classroom-wrong: #9f1239;
  --classroom-unsure: #b45309;
  --classroom-correct: #047857;
  --classroom-radius: 8px;
  --classroom-transition: 300ms;
  align-items: flex-start;
  background: var(--classroom-page);
  color: var(--classroom-ink);
  display: flex;
  font-family: "Outfit", ui-sans-serif, system-ui, sans-serif;
  font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums;
  font-weight: 500;
  min-height: 100dvh;
  transition:
    background-color var(--classroom-transition) ease,
    color var(--classroom-transition) ease;
  width: 100%;
}

html.dark .classroom-root {
  --classroom-line: #17161c;
  --classroom-ink: #fffffe;
  --classroom-ink-body: #e5e5e5;
  --classroom-meta: 167, 169, 190;
  --classroom-hover: rgba(68, 64, 60, 0.4);
  --classroom-dash: rgba(255, 255, 255, 0.15);
  --classroom-active: #3d3c48;
  --classroom-active-ink: #e5e5e5;
  --classroom-meta-soft: #a7a9be;
  /* On the dark page both base values already clear 8:1, so the text-safe pair
     is the same colour here. Declared so the token means one thing everywhere. */
  --classroom-link-text: #ff8906;

/**
 * One practice test, in progress.
 *
 * This page is the invigilator, not the exam. It says which section is next,
 * starts it, runs the break, reads each leg's result back out of the tables the
 * runners write, and scores the sitting at the end. Every actual question is
 * answered somewhere else, in the same runner the student uses outside a test.
 *
 * The result readback is deliberately conservative: a leg with no matching row
 * is reported as unfinished rather than scored as zero. A zero here would read
 * on the score report as "answered everything wrong", which is a different and
 * much more discouraging claim than "you walked away from section 2".
 */
import * as React from "react";
import { useNavigate, useParams } from "react-router-dom";
import { Loader2 } from "lucide-react";
import { Button } from "@/components/ui/button";
import { useAuth } from "@/contexts/AuthContext";
import {
  formatPracticeTestClock,
  legLabel,
  loadPracticeTestRun,
  practiceTestPace,
  practiceTestRaw,
  practiceTestScaled,
  savePracticeTestRun,

  type PracticeTestRun as Run,
} from "@/lib/practiceTest";
import { lrLegLaunch, rcLegHref, readLegResult } from "@/lib/practiceTestRunner";
import "./testing-centre.css";

function mmss(seconds: number) {
  const s = Math.max(0, Math.floor(seconds));
  return `${Math.floor(s / 60)}:${String(s % 60).padStart(2, "0")}`;
}

/** The break, on its own clock. Skippable — it is a break, not a gate. */
function BreakScreen({ run, onDone }: { run: Run; onDone: () => void }) {
  const startedAt = run.breakStartedAt ? Date.parse(run.breakStartedAt) : Date.now();
  const [remaining, setRemaining] = React.useState(() =>
    Math.max(0, PRACTICE_TEST_BREAK_SECONDS - (Date.now() - startedAt) / 1000),
  );

  React.useEffect(() => {
    const id = window.setInterval(() => {
      setRemaining(Math.max(0, PRACTICE_TEST_BREAK_SECONDS - (Date.now() - startedAt) / 1000));
    }, 500);
    return () => window.clearInterval(id);
  }, [startedAt]);

  return (
    <div className="testing-panel">
      <div className="testing-score">
        <strong>{mmss(remaining)}</strong>
        <span>break remaining</span>
      </div>
      <p className="testing-note">
        Stand up, get water, look at something further away than a screen. The
        real test gives you this, and taking it is part of practising the
        sitting rather than just the questions.
      </p>
      <div className="testing-actions">
        <Button onClick={onDone}>
          {remaining > 0 ? "Skip the break and continue" : "Continue"}
        </Button>
      </div>
    </div>
  );
}

function ScoreReport({ run, onExit }: { run: Run; onExit: () => void }) {

  const scaled = practiceTestScaled(run);
  const unfinished = run.legs.filter((l) => !l.completedAt);

        </span>
      </div>
      <p className="testing-note" style={{ marginTop: 12 }}>
        Estimated from a typical modern LSAT curve, not PrepTest {run.pt}'s own
        conversion table. Treat it as a band, not a number.
      </p>

      <div className="testing-shape">
        {run.legs.map((leg, i) => (

            <em>
              {leg.completedAt
                ? `${leg.correct ?? 0}/${leg.questionCount}`
                : "not finished"}
            </em>
          </div>
        ))}
      </div>

      {unfinished.length > 0 && (
        <p className="testing-note">
          {unfinished.length === 1 ? "One section was" : `${unfinished.lengt...

export function savePracticeTestRun(run: PracticeTestRun): void {
  try {
    localStorage.setItem(storeKey(run.id), JSON.stringify(run));
  } catch {
    // A full or disabled store must not take the sitting down. The student
    // keeps answering; only resume-after-reload is lost.
  }
}

export function loadPracticeTestRun(id: string): PracticeTestRun | null {
  try {
    const raw = localStorage.getItem(storeKey(id));
    if (!raw) return null;
    const parsed = JSON.parse(raw) as PracticeTestRun;
    if (!parsed || !Array.isArray(parsed.legs)) return null;
    return parsed;
  } catch {
    return null;
  }
}

export function listPracticeTestRuns(): PracticeTestRun[] {
  const runs: PracticeTestRun[] = [];
  try {
    for (let i = 0; i < localStorage.length; i += 1) {
      const key = localStorage.key(i);
      if (!key || !key.startsWith(STORE_PREFIX)) continue;
      const run = loadPracticeTestRun(key.slice(STORE_PREFIX.length));
      if (run) runs.push(run);
    }
  } catch {
    return [];
  }
  return runs.sort((a, b) => b.createdAt.localeCompare(a.createdAt));
}

export function deletePracticeTestRun(id: string): void {
  try {
    localStorage.removeItem(storeKey(id));

  breakTaken: boolean;
  /** ISO stamp set when the break clock started, so it survives a reload. */
  breakStartedAt?: string;
  status: PracticeTestStatus;
  createdAt: string;
}

export interface PracticeTestBlueprint {
  pt: number;

  /**
   * False when the Reading Comprehension leg carries fewer questions than a
   * real section does. The sitting is still worth doing — the reading load is
   * identical and reading is what the clock is really measuring — but the raw
   * total is smaller, and every surface that offers this has to say so.
   */
  rcFullLength: boolean;
}

## First, two things that happened before the audit

**The dev build was broken at HEAD.** `src/components/questionfinder/questionFinder.css:119` had a dangling selector (`.qf-trigger-label,` with nothing after it) committed in `f4cf062`. PostCSS threw, the Vite overlay covered the whole app, and no route rendered. I completed the rule to `.qf-trigger-label { display: none; }`, which is what the surrounding comment describes. That file is now dirty in your tree — review it, it isn't mine to own.

**Another session is live on port 8080** and editing `SectionReportCard.tsx`, so the page reloaded under me several times. I confirmed the panel state itself is stable (12s poll, no drift) — the collapses were HMR, not a defect. I also created and removed a temporary `.env.local`, and seeded then deleted two throwaway localStorage runs (`audit-tmp`, `audit-tmp2`). Nothing else of mine is left behind.

## Audit Health Score

| # | Dimension | Score | Key finding |
|---|-----------|-------|-------------|
| 1 | Accessibility | 1 | Current-section row renders at **1.00:1** — text is literally invisible |
| 2 | Performance | 3 | Whole RC index loads on lobby mount; a blur that blurs nothing |
| 3 | Responsive | 3 | Clean at 375 and 1440; 670px-wide selects are a measure problem, not a break |
| 4 | Theming | 1 | `var(--classroom-meta)` is a channel triple used as a colour — invalid, silently falls back to ink |
| 5 | Anti-Patterns | 2 | Nested cards + glassmorphism, both imported from `SectionSelector` |
| **Total** | | **10/20** | **Acceptable — significant work needed** |

---

## Anti-Patterns Verdict: **pass, with an imported failure**

The room you wrote does not look AI-generated. Two doors instead of a card grid, a hairline table for the sitting's shape, no gradient, no hero-metric on the lobby, no modal — it reads as a deliberate lobby, which is what the file comment says it's for.

The failure is entirely in what it wraps. `.testing-panel` opens and renders `SectionSelector`, which is a **glass card inside your card**: `bg-background/80 backdrop-blur-xl border-border/50 rounded-2xl shadow-[0_8px_30px...]` at `SectionSelector.tsx:298`. Three tells at once — nested cards, decorative glassmorphism, and a drop shadow on a system whose own DESIGN.md says "flat surfaces, hairline separation, no drop shadows."

It also brings a second token system onto the screen: `rounded-2xl` (16px) against your `--classroom-radius` (8px), neutral `rgba(224,224,224,0.5)` against your warm `#f3e3d6`. The two cards visibly disagree about what product they're in.

**And you own this outright.** The comment at the top of `TestingCentre.tsx` says the section door "hands off to the launcher the drill hub already uses." It doesn't any more — `SectionSelector` is imported by exactly one production file, yours. There is no shared consumer to negotiate with. The component still behaves like a standalone page (its own `<h1>`, its own `p-12` glass card, a centred header in a left-aligned room), and nothing stops you from flattening it.

---

## Executive Summary

- **10/20**, Acceptable
- **P0: 1 · P1: 6 · P2: 5 · P3: 3**

Top five:

1. The current-section row is invisible in light mode (1.00:1).
2. Every piece of secondary text on the surface is either 2.10:1 or wrongly rendered at full ink — the token layer is broken in two different directions at once.
3. The score report's honesty caveat is the faintest text on the screen while the number it qualifies is 56px.
4. `SectionSelector` nests a glass card in a card and puts a second `<h1>` on the page.
5. The doors use `aria-pressed` for a disclosure, and neither door is connected to the panel it opens.

---

## P0 — Blocking

### [P0] The current section row is invisible
**Location:** `src/pages/testing-centre.css:145`, rendered by `src/pages/PracticeTestRun.tsx:255`
**Category:** Accessibility / Theming
**Measured:** row background `rgb(67,64,60)`; `b`, `span` and `em` all resolve to `rgb(67,64,60)`. **Contrast 1.00:1 on all three.** Screenshot shows a solid black bar where "SECTION 2 · Logical Reasoning · 26 questions" should be.

```
.testing-shape-row[data-state='current'] { background: var(--classroom-active); }
```

In light, `--classroom-active` is `#43403c` and `--classroom-ink` is `#43403c`. The row paints the ink colour behind ink-coloured text. Dark mode is fine (`#3d3c48` behind `#fffffe`), so this ships broken only in the default theme.

**Impact:** this is the row that tells the student which section is next on a run they're mid-way through. It is the single most load-bearing line on the page and it renders as a black bar.
**WCAG:** 1.4.3 (AA) — catastrophic failure.
**Fix:** `--classroom-active-ink: #ffffff` already exists for exactly this and is never used. Set it on the row's `b`, `span` and `em`.
**Command:** `$impeccable harden`

---

## P1 — Major

### [P1] `--classroom-meta` is a channel triple used as a colour
**Location:** `testing-centre.css:11` (`.testing-lede`), `:64` (`.testing-door p`), `:175` (`.testing-score span`)
**Category:** Theming

`classroom.css:5` defines `--classroom-meta: 166, 162, 158` — bare channels, consumed everywhere else in `classroom.css` as `rgb(var(--classroom-meta))` (10+ call sites). `testing-centre.css` writes `color: var(--classroom-meta)` without the wrapper. That produces `color: 166, 162, 158`, which is invalid at computed-value time, so the declaration is dropped and the element inherits ink.

**Verified at runtime:** `.testing-lede` computes to `rgb(67, 64, 60)` — identical to the h1 above it. Same in dark (`rgb(255,255,254)`).

**Impact:** the lede, the door descriptions and the score-report label all render at full ink. The page has no tonal hierarchy at all; title, subtitle and body copy differ only by size. It looks flat and slightly shouty, and nobody would ever guess the cause from looking.

This is DESIGN.md's Named Ground Rule, verbatim: *"a token that resolves to nothing is a defect, not a subtle effect."* The comment at `classroom.css:9-14` even names the trap and provides the answer.
**Fix:** use `var(--classroom-meta-text)` (`#6d6a67`, 5.39:1 on white, documented as the text-safe half of the pair) in all three places.
**Command:** `$impeccable harden`

### [P1] Five text roles render at 2.10:1
**Location:** `testing-centre.css:71, 133, 150, 154`
**Category:** Accessibility
**Measured on the live page (light):**

| Element | Text | Size | Contrast |
|---|---|---|---|
| `.testing-door-meta` | ONE SECTION · 35 MINUTES | 11px | **2.10:1** |
| `.testing-shape-row em` | 26 questions · 35:00 | 12.5px | **2.10:1** |
| `.testing-note` | "79 scored questions, with a ten-minute break…" | 12.5px | **2.10:1** |
| `.testing-note` | "Estimated from a typical modern LSAT curve…" | 12.5px | **2.10:1** |
| `[data-state=break] b, span` | break row | 11px | **2.10:1** |

All four rules use `--classroom-meta-soft`, which in light is `rgba(67,64,60,0.4)`. Dark is fine (`#a7a9be`, 8.54:1), so this too is light-only.

**Impact:** the clock is the entire premise of this room, and `35 MINUTES` on the door plus `35:00` in the shape table are both at 2.1:1. The code comment at `TestingCentre.tsx:274` says *"No clock here: the meta line below already carries it"* — the meta line is the least legible thing on the door.
**WCAG:** 1.4.3 (AA), 4.5:1 required. Off by more than half.
**Fix:** `--classroom-meta-soft` is a decorative token (rules, dividers). Text goes to `--classroom-meta-text`.
**Command:** `$impeccable harden`

### [P1] The truthfulness caveat is the faintest thing on the score report
**Location:** `PracticeTestRun.tsx:80` vs `:85`
**Category:** Anti-Pattern / Accessibility

`158` renders at 56px/700. Directly under it, at 12.5px and 2.10:1: *"Estimated from a typical modern LSAT curve, not PrepTest 103's own conversion table. Treat it as a band, not a number."*

The copy asks the student to read it as a band. The typography asks them to read it as a number, and hides the sentence that says otherwise. PRODUCT.md principle 1 is truthfulness over performance; here the visual hierarchy inverts it. This is the finding I'd fix second, after the invisible row.

Same shape at `PracticeTestRun.tsx:321` — "Next: Logical Reasoning, 26 questions, 35:00 on the clock" is the only sentence explaining what the button below it does, and it's at 2.1:1.
**Fix:** the caveat wants `--classroom-meta-text` at 13–14px sitting *next to* the number, not under it in fine print. If it's true enough to write, it's true enough to read.
**Command:** `$impeccable typeset`

### [P1] `SectionSelector` nests a glass card and a second `<h1>`
**Location:** `TestingCentre.tsx:301` → `SectionSelector.tsx:298, 301`
**Category:** Anti-Pattern / Accessibility
**Measured:** outer `.testing-panel` 720px, `#ffffff`, 1px `#f3e3d6`, 24px padding. Inner 638px, `rgba(252,252,252,0.8)`, `backdrop-blur-xl`, `rounded-2xl`, `0 8px 30px rgba(0,0,0,0.04)`.

Three problems in one element: nested cards (an absolute ban), decorative glassmorphism, and a heading structure that goes `h1` → `h2` → `h2` → `h1`. Two `<h1>`s on one page breaks document structure for every screen reader and every heading-navigation user.

The copy repeats too. The door says "Take a section / One real LR or RC section from an official PrepTest." The panel it opens says "Full Section / Sit a real section, start to finish." Same statement, twice, 40px apart.
**Fix:** strip the wrapper card, the shadow, the blur and the centred header from `SectionSelector`; demote its `h1` to `h2` or drop it (the door already named the thing). It has one consumer — nothing else breaks.
**Command:** `$impeccable distill`

### [P1] `aria-pressed` on a disclosure, and no link to the panel
**Location:** `TestingCentre.tsx:269, 281`
**Category:** Accessibility

`aria-pressed` announces a toggle button as "pressed / not pressed." These doors expand a panel; the correct pair is `aria-expanded` + `aria-controls` pointing at the panel's id. As written, a screen reader user hears "pressed," a panel appears somewhere below with no announced relationship, and nothing tells them where it went.

Sighted users have the same problem in weaker form: the open door gets a darker border, but the panel is a detached card below *both* doors with no visual tether. The scroll-into-view at `:238` is a workaround for a relationship the layout never establishes. (Credit where due — that effect respects `prefers-reduced-motion`.)
**WCAG:** 4.1.2.
**Fix:** `aria-expanded={door === "section"}`, `aria-controls="testing-panel"`, drop `aria-pressed`.
**Command:** `$impeccable harden`

### [P1] The break countdown is silent to assistive tech
**Location:** `PracticeTestRun.tsx:46-56`
**Category:** Accessibility

`mmss(remaining)` updates inside a plain `<strong>` every 500ms with no `aria-live` and no `role="timer"`. Across both files the entire ARIA vocabulary is `aria-pressed` ×2 and `aria-hidden` ×4 — there is no live region anywhere, so "Loading questions…", "Reading that section's result…" and "Working out which PrepTests can be sat end to end…" are all silent state changes too.

PRODUCT.md is explicit: *"Screen readers are informed of state that changes without a page load."*
**Fix:** `role="timer" aria-live="polite"` on the countdown announcing at a coarse interval (each minute, then each 10s under a minute — not twice a second). `aria-live="polite"` or `aria-busy` on the three loading states.
**Command:** `$impeccable harden`

---

## P2 — Minor

### [P2] Headings and emphasis used as layout
`TestingCentre.tsx:272, 284` put `<h2>` and `<p>` inside a `<button>`. `<button>` takes phrasing content only, so this is invalid HTML — and it explains why the doors dropped out of the accessibility tree entirely on one of my reads. It also means heading navigation lands users on a control.

`TestingCentre.tsx:194` wraps the Resume and Discard buttons in `<em style={{display:"flex",gap:8}}>` — stress emphasis used as a flex container. Same misuse in `.testing-shape-row`, where `<b>`/`<span>`/`<em>` are three columns of a table.
**Fix:** `<span class="testing-door-title">` in the button; a `<div>` for the action group; the shape rows want a real table or a definition list.
**Command:** `$impeccable harden`

### [P2] The doors' boundary is 1.22:1
`--classroom-panel` and `--classroom-page` are both `#ffffff` in light, so a door's only edge is the `#f3e3d6` hairline at **1.22:1**. WCAG 1.4.11 asks 3:1 for the visual boundary of an interactive control. Arguable — the text inside identifies the content — but the *clickable extent* is genuinely hard to see, and these are 352px targets that start a clock. Worth 3:1 on that ground alone.
**Command:** `$impeccable colorize`

### [P2] `<h3>` before `<h2>`
`ResumeList` renders at `TestingCentre.tsx:182` with an `<h3>`, above the doors' `<h2>`s at `:272`. Reading order is h1 → h3 → h2 → h2.
**Command:** `$impeccable harden`

### [P2] The score report shows the same table twice
`PracticeTestRun.tsx:253` renders the leg list, then `:88` renders it again inside the score panel. On a finished sitting the first copy reads "Finished / Finished / Finished" and carries nothing the second doesn't say better. Two near-identical three-row tables stacked.
**Command:** `$impeccable distill`

### [P2] A 59-option flat list, and 670px-wide selects
The PrepTest dropdown holds 59 items with no search, no grouping and no recency — every student scrolls from 101 to find what they want. Meanwhile both selects stretch to 670px inside a 720px panel to hold "PrepTest 103 · 79 questions." The form is dramatically wider than its content, and the panel is wide because it spans two doors, not because anything in it needs the room.
**Command:** `$impeccable layout`

---

## P3 — Polish

- **[P3] The lobby loads the entire RC index to render two buttons.** `useRcSections` calls `loadAllIndexPassages()` unconditionally at mount (`TestingCentre.tsx:52-54`); only the practice-test door needs it. Defer to `door === "test"`.
- **[P3] `backdrop-blur-xl` blurs nothing.** The glass card sits on an opaque white panel, so the blur costs a compositing layer and produces no visible effect.
- **[P3] Inline styles.** `style={{marginTop: 0}}` appears four times across the two files, plus `style={{display:"flex",gap:8}}`. Each is a rule the stylesheet should own.

---

## Patterns & systemic issues

**The token layer fails in two opposite directions on one surface.** `--classroom-meta` is too dark (falls back to ink); `--classroom-meta-soft` is too light (2.1:1). Both have the same root cause: `testing-centre.css` treats `classroom.css`'s tokens as interchangeable colours when they're a documented set with specific jobs, and the one token built for this exact purpose — `--classroom-meta-text` — is never used. Every other classroom surface got this right. Worth a repo-wide grep for `color: var(--classroom-meta)` and `color: var(--classroom-meta-soft)` before you fix this one file.

**Light mode carries every contrast failure.** Dark measured clean throughout: 19.16:1, 19.77:1, 8.54:1, and the current-section row works. Light is the shipped default, so the whole surface's a11y debt sits in the theme almost everyone sees.

**Structural markup is decorative.** `h2` in a button, `h1` in a panel, `em` as a flex container, `b`/`span`/`em` as table columns. Chosen for how they render, not what they mean — which is why the accessibility tree for this page is unreliable.

---

## What's working

- **The two-door concept is genuinely good.** It refuses the card grid, it separates a sitting from a drill for a stated pedagogical reason, and the file comment explains why. That's the surface's own design and it holds up.
- **The shape table earns its place.** Showing Section 1/2/3 with counts and clock before the student commits is exactly the "compute the next step" principle, and a hairline table was the right form for it.
- **The honesty is real.** The short-RC disclosure, "not finished" instead of a zero, and the curve caveat are all present and all correct. They're just typeset as if they were disclaimers rather than the product.
- **`prefers-reduced-motion` is respected** in the panel scroll, and the focus ring works — verified with real Tab presses (`solid 2px #121212`, 2px offset). I checked `outline: none` first with programmatic focus and it was a false alarm; keyboard focus is fine.
- **Responsive is solid.** No horizontal overflow at 375px, doors stack cleanly at 900px, every control measured ≥44px.

---

## Recommended actions

1. **[P0] `$impeccable harden`** — the invisible current-section row. `--classroom-active-ink` already exists; ten minutes.
2. **[P1] `$impeccable harden`** — the token pass: `--classroom-meta` → `--classroom-meta-text` in three places, `--classroom-meta-soft` → `--classroom-meta-text` in four. Fixes both the missing hierarchy and all five 2.1:1 failures at once.
3. **[P1] `$impeccable distill`** — flatten `SectionSelector`: drop the glass card, the shadow, the second `h1`, the centred header, the restated copy. One consumer, no blast radius.
4. **[P1] `$impeccable harden`** — ARIA: `aria-expanded`/`aria-controls` on the doors, `role="timer"` on the break clock, live regions on the three loading states.
5. **[P1] `$impeccable typeset`** — promote the curve caveat next to the score; it's the most important sentence on that screen.
6. **[P2] `$impeccable layout`** — panel width, select measure, and searchable PrepTest picking.
7. **[P2] `$impeccable harden`** — semantic markup: headings out of buttons, `em` out of layout, `h3`/`h2` order.
8. **[P3] `$impeccable optimize`** — defer the RC index to the practice-test door.
9. **[P3] `$impeccable polish`** — final pass.

You can ask me to run these one at a time, all at once, or in any order you prefer. Re-run `$impeccable audit` after fixes to see the score move.

Two open items for you: the `questionFinder.css` fix is uncommitted in your tree and belongs to whoever made `f4cf062`, and another session is holding port 8080. Want me to start on the P0 and the token pass?

function ResumeList({
  runs,
  onResume,
  onDiscard,
}: {
  runs: PracticeTestRun[];
  onResume: (run: PracticeTestRun) => void;
  onDiscard: (run: PracticeTestRun) => void;
}) {
  if (runs.length === 0) return null;
  return (
    <div className="testing-resume">
      <h3>Unfinished sittings</h3>
      <div className="testing-shape">
        {runs.map((run) => {
          return (
            <div className="testing-shape-row" key={run.id}>
              <b>PT {run.pt}</b>
              <span>
                Section {Math.min(run.currentLeg + 1, run.legs.length)} of{" "}
                {run.legs.length}

              </span>
              <em style={{ display: "flex", gap: 8 }}>
                <Button size="sm" variant="outline" onClick={() => onResume(run)}>
                  Resume
                </Button>
                <Button size="sm" variant="ghost" onClick={() => onDiscard(run)}>
                  Discard
                </Button>
              </em>
            </div>
          );
        })}
      </div>
    </div>
  );
}

export default function TestingCentre() {
  const navigate = useNavigate();
  const { manifest, isLoading, error } = useQuestionBank();

  const [runs, setRuns] = React.useState<PracticeTestRun[]>([]);

/**

 *
 * The interesting case is the short Reading Comprehension section. An earlier
 * cut of this feature refused those PrepTests outright, which left 11 of 58
 * sittable and read on screen as "we only have 11 tests". They are offered now,
 * with the shortfall stated — so the test that matters is that the statement
 * appears exactly when it is true and not when it is not.
 */
import { fireEvent, render, screen, waitFor } from "@testing-library/react";
import { MemoryRouter } from "react-router-dom";
import { beforeAll, describe, expect, it, vi } from "vitest";
import TestingCentre from "./TestingCentre";

const navigate = vi.fn();
vi.mock("react-router-dom", async () => {
  const actual = await vi.importActual<typeof import("react-router-dom")>("react-router-dom");
  return { ...actual, useNavigate: () => navigate };
});

vi.mock("@/contexts/QuestionBankContext", () => ({
  useQuestionBank: () => ({
    manifest: {
      sections: [
        { pt: 130, section: 1, count: 25, filename: "PT130-S1-LR.json" },
        { pt: 130, section: 3, count: 25, filename: "PT130-S3-LR.json" },
        { pt: 131, section: 1, count: 25, filename: "PT131-S1-LR.json" },
        { pt: 131, section: 3, count: 26, filename: "PT131-S3-LR.json" },
      ],
    },
    isLoading: false,
    error: null,
  }),
}));

/** PT 130 has a short RC section; PT 131 has a full one. */
const passage = (preptest: number, passageId: number, setNumber: number, questionCount: number) => ({
  passageId,
  section: 4,
  setNumber,
  title: `Passage ${setNumber}`,
  topic: "Science",
  format: "Analysis",
  frameworks: [],
  difficulty: 3,
  questionCount,
  explainedCount: questionCount,
  questionIds: Array.from({ length: questionCount }, (_, i) => passageId * 100 + i),

 * layout and capture APIs jsdom does not provide; its keyboard path is the same
 * open, and is the one a screen-reader user takes anyway.
 */

  const trigger = screen.getByRole("combobox", { name });
  trigger.focus();
  fireEvent.keyDown(trigger, { key: "Enter", code: "Enter" });
};

/**
 * What the section launcher tells a student before they commit.
 *
 * Two pieces of copy are load-bearing here. "Fresh" is our word for a section
 * with no attempts against it, and on its own it is a filter nobody can read.
 * And every field on this panel states a choice while none of them says what
 * happens once the choice is made — a student pressing Start is committing to
 * the whole section under a clock, and should know that before they press it.
 *
 * The commitment line has to be conditional in two directions: it cannot quote
 * a question count before a section is chosen, and it cannot promise a clock
 * that Unlimited does not start.
 */

}));

  const actual =
    await vi.importActual<typeof import('@/lib/sectionFreshness')>('@/lib/sectionFreshness');

});

  },
}));

  loadAllIndexPassages: () => Promise.resolve([]),
}));

/** Routed: the timing popover carries the accommodations note, which links out. */
const renderSelector = () =>
  render(
    <MemoryRouter>
      <SectionSelector manifest={manifest} onStartSection={vi.fn()} onCancel={vi.fn()} />
    </MemoryRouter>,
  );

/**
 * The panel's labels are not tied to their triggers, so a combobox here has no
 * accessible name to query by. Each trigger is found through the text it is
 * currently showing instead, and opened by keyboard: Radix's pointer path needs
 * layout and capture APIs jsdom does not provide, and the keyboard path is the
 * same open.
 */
const openSelectShowing = async (text: RegExp) => {
  const trigger = (await screen.findByText(text)).closest('[role="combobox"]') as HTMLElement;
  trigger.focus();
  fireEvent.keyDown(trigger, { key: 'Enter', code: 'Enter' });
};

const pickSection = async () => {
  await openSelectShowing(/Select a PrepTest/);
  fireEvent.click(await screen.findByText(/^PrepTest 130$/));
  await openSelectShowing(/Select a section/);
  fireEvent.click(await screen.findByText(/^Section 3$/));
};

import "@testing-library/jest-dom";

// Supabase auth client reads localStorage on init; provide a full implementation
// so it doesn't emit unhandled rejections in the jsdom test environment.
const _localStore: Record<string, string> = {};
const localStorageMock: Storage = {
  getItem:    (k) => _localStore[k] ?? null,
  setItem:    (k, v) => { _localStore[k] = String(v); },
  removeItem: (k) => { delete _localStore[k]; },
  clear:      () => { Object.keys(_localStore).forEach((k) => delete _localStore[k]); },
  get length() { return Object.keys(_localStore).length; },
  key:        (i) => Object.keys(_localStore)[i] ?? null,
};
Object.defineProperty(window, "localStorage", { value: localStorageMock, writable: true });

// jsdom ships no IntersectionObserver. Anything that lazily renders when it
// scrolls into view (library card previews, the carousel) throws on mount
// without this. Nothing is ever "intersecting" here, so components that
// observe visibility stay in their off-screen state unless a test drives them.
class IntersectionObserverMock implements IntersectionObserver {
  readonly root = null;
  readonly rootMargin = "";
  readonly scrollMargin = "";
  readonly thresholds: ReadonlyArray<number> = [];
  observe() {}
  unobserve() {}
  disconnect() {}
  takeRecords(): IntersectionObserverEntry[] { return []; }
}
// configurable so an individual test can still swap in its own spy.
for (const target of [window, globalThis]) {
  Object.defineProperty(target, "IntersectionObserver", {
    writable: true,
    configurable: true,
    value: IntersectionObserverMock,
  });
}

// Same story for ResizeObserver, which the carousel uses to measure slides.
class ResizeObserverMock implements ResizeObserver {
  observe() {}
  unobserve() {}
  disconnect() {}
}
for (const target of [window, globalThis]) {
  Object.defineProperty(target, "ResizeObserver", {
    writable: true,
    configurable: true,
    value: ResizeObserverMock,
  });
}

Object.defineProperty(window, "matchMedia", {
  writable: true,
  value: (query: string) => ({
    matches: false,
    media: query,
    onchange: null,
    addListener: () => {},
    removeListener: () => {},
    addEventListener: () => {},
    removeEventListener: () => {},
    dispatchEvent: () => {},
  }),
});

import * as React from "react";
import * as PopoverPrimitive from "@radix-ui/react-popover";

import { cn } from "@/lib/utils";

const Popover = PopoverPrimitive.Root;

const PopoverTrigger = PopoverPrimitive.Trigger;

const PopoverContent = React.forwardRef<
  React.ElementRef<typeof PopoverPrimitive.Content>,
  React.ComponentPropsWithoutRef<typeof PopoverPrimitive.Content>
>(({ className, align = "center", sideOffset = 4, ...props }, ref) => (
  <PopoverPrimitive.Portal>
    <PopoverPrimitive.Content
      ref={ref}
      align={align}
      sideOffset={sideOffset}
      className={cn(
        "z-[1000] w-72 rounded-md border bg-popover p-4 text-popover-foreground shadow-md outline-none data-[state=open]:animate-in data-[state=closed]:animate-out data-[state=closed]:fade-out-0 data-[state=open]:fade-in-0 data-[state=closed]:zoom-out-95 data-[state=open]:zoom-in-95 data-[side=bottom]:slide-in-from-top-2 data-[side=left]:slide-in-from-right-2 data-[side=right]:slide-in-from-left-2 data-[side=top]:slide-in-from-bottom-2",
        className,
      )}
      {...props}
    />
  </PopoverPrimitive.Portal>
));
PopoverContent.displayName = PopoverPrimitive.Content.displayName;

export { Popover, PopoverTrigger, PopoverContent };

command: python - <<'PYEOF'
import io
p='src/pages/PracticeTestRun.tsx'
s=io.open(p,encoding='utf-8').read()

# ---------- 1. BreakScreen: real timer semantics, and stop ticking at zero ----------
old = '''/** The break, on its own clock. Skippable — it is a break, not a gate. */
function BreakScreen({ run, onDone }: { run: Run; onDone: () => void }) {
  const startedAt = run.breakStartedAt ? Date.parse(run.breakStartedAt) : Date.now();
  const [remaining, setRemaining] = React.useState(() =>
    Math.max(0, PRACTICE_TEST_BREAK_SECONDS - (Date.now() - startedAt) / 1000),
  );

  React.useEffect(() => {
    const id = window.setInterval(() => {
      setRemaining(Math.max(0, PRACTICE_TEST_BREAK_SECONDS - (Date.now() - startedAt) / 1000));
    }, 500);
    return () => window.clearInterval(id);
  }, [startedAt]);

  return (
    <div className="testing-panel">
      <div className="testing-score">
        <strong>{mmss(remaining)}</strong>
        <span>break remaining</span>
      </div>
      <p className="testing-note">
        Stand up, get water, look at something further away than a screen. The
        real test gives you this, and taking it is part of practising the
        sitting rather than just the questions.
      </p>
      <div className="testing-actions">
        <Button onClick={onDone}>
          {remaining > 0 ? "Skip the break and continue" : "Continue"}
        </Button>
      </div>
    </div>
  );
}'''

new = '''/**
 * What a ticking clock says out loud.
 *
 * The digits change twice a second, and a live region wired straight to them
 * would talk over itself continuously for ten minutes. Coarse steps instead:
 * whole minutes while there are minutes left, then ten-second steps in the last
 * one. The string only changes when the step does, so the region only speaks
 * when there is something new to say.
 */
function breakAnnouncement(remaining: number): string {
  const seconds = Math.ceil(remaining);
  if (seconds <= 0) return "The break is over. Continue when you are ready.";
  if (seconds <= 60) return `${Math.ceil(seconds / 10) * 10} seconds of the break left.`;
  const minutes = Math.ceil(seconds / 60);
  return `${minutes} ${minutes === 1 ? "minute" : "minutes"} of the break left.`;
}

/** The break, on its own clock. Skippable — it is a break, not a gate. */
function BreakScreen({ run, onDone }: { run: Run; onDone: () => void }) {
  const startedAt = run.breakStartedAt ? Date.parse(run.breakStartedAt) : Date.now();
  const [remaining, setRemaining] = React.useState(() =>
    Math.max(0, PRACTICE_TEST_BREAK_SECONDS - (Date.now() - startedAt) / 1000),
  );

  React.useEffect(() => {
    if (remaining <= 0) return;
    const id = window.setInterval(() => {
      setRemaining(Math.max(0, PRACTICE_TEST_BREAK_SECONDS - (Date.now() - startedAt) / 1000));
    }, 500);
    return () => window.clearInterval(id);
  }, [startedAt, remaining]);

  return (
    <div className="testing-panel">
      <h2 className="testing-heading">Break</h2>
      <div className="testing-score">
        {/* The digits are for the eye. Assistive tech is given the coarse
            announcement below instead of a number that changes 1,200 times. */}
        <strong aria-hidden>{mmss(remaining)}</strong>
        <span aria-hidden>break remaining</span>
        <span className="sr-only" role="timer" aria-live="polite">
          {breakAnnouncement(remaining)}
        </span>
      </div>
      <p className="testing-note">
        Stand up, get water, look at something further away than a screen. The
        real test gives you this, and taking it is part of practising the
        sitting rather than just the questions.
      </p>
      <div className="testing-actions">
        <Button onClick={onDone}>
          {remaining > 0 ? "Skip the break and continue" : "Continue"}
        </Button>
      </div>
    </div>
  );
}'''
assert s.count(old) == 1, "BreakScreen anchor"
s = s.replace(old, new, 1)

# ---------- 2. ScoreReport: heading, promoted caveat ----------
old = '''  return (
    <div className="testing-panel">
      <div className="testing-score">
        <strong>{scaled}</strong>
        <span>
          estimated score · {correct} of {total} correct

      </div>
      <p className="testing-note" style={{ marginTop: 12 }}>
        Estimated from a typical modern LSAT curve, not PrepTest {run.pt}'s own
        conversion table. Treat it as a band, not a number.

'''

[external_agent_tool_call: Bash]
description: Wire SectionSelector accessible names
command: python - <<'PY'
import io, re
p = 'src/components/drill/SectionSelector.tsx'
s = io.open(p, encoding='utf-8').read()

# Give every label an id, at whatever indentation it already sits on.
s, n1 = re.subn(
    r'(\n(\s+))htmlFor="([a-z]+-select)"',
    lambda m: f'{m.group(1)}htmlFor="{m.group(3)}"{m.group(1)}id="{m.group(3)}-label"',
    s,
)

# Point each trigger at its label. <label for> does not name a <button> under
# HTML-AAM, so these comboboxes announced their value and never their field.
# The self-reference keeps the value in the announcement.
s, n2 = re.subn(
    r'(\n(\s+))className="text-sm"(\n\s+)id="([a-z]+-select)"',
    lambda m: (f'{m.group(1)}aria-labelledby="{m.group(4)}-label {m.group(4)}"'
               f'{m.group(1)}className="text-sm"{m.group(3)}id="{m.group(4)}"'),
    s,
)

assert n1 == 3, n1
assert n2 == 3, n2
io.open(p, 'w', encoding='utf-8', newline='').write(s)
print(f'SectionSelector: {n1} label ids, {n2} trigger names')
PY
sed -n '353,372p' src/components/drill/SectionSelector.tsx

import { fireEvent, render, screen, waitFor } from "@testing-library/react";
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import {
  Command,
  CommandEmpty,
  CommandInput,
  CommandItem,
  CommandList,
} from "@/components/ui/command";

describe("cmdk in jsdom", () => {
  it("filters", async () => {
    render(
      <Command>
        <CommandInput placeholder="Search by number" />
        <CommandList>
          <CommandEmpty>none</CommandEmpty>
          <CommandItem value="PrepTest 130">PrepTest 130 · 69 questions</CommandItem>
          <CommandItem value="PrepTest 131">PrepTest 131 · 78 questions</CommandItem>
        </CommandList>
      </Command>,
    );
    fireEvent.change(screen.getByPlaceholderText(/Search by number/i), {
      target: { value: "131" },
    });
    await waitFor(() => {
      const items = Array.from(document.querySelectorAll("[cmdk-item]")).map((el) => ({
        text: el.textContent,
        hidden: el.getAttribute("hidden"),
        ariaHidden: el.getAttribute("aria-hidden"),
        display: (el as HTMLElement).style.display,
      }));
      console.log(JSON.stringify(items, null, 1));
      expect(items.length).toBeGreaterThan(0);
    });
  });
});
EOF

description: Probe cmdk filtering with mocks
command: cat > src/pages/__cmdk_probe.test.tsx <<'EOF'
import { fireEvent, render, screen, waitFor } from "@testing-library/react";
import { beforeAll, describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import {
  Command,
  CommandEmpty,
  CommandInput,
  CommandItem,
  CommandList,
} from "@/components/ui/command";

beforeAll(() => {
  const proto = window.HTMLElement.prototype as unknown as Record<string, unknown>;
  proto.scrollIntoView = () => undefined;
});

describe("cmdk in jsdom", () => {
  it("filters", async () => {
    render(
      <Command>
        <CommandInput placeholder="Search by number" />
        <CommandList>
          <CommandEmpty>none</CommandEmpty>
          <CommandItem value="PrepTest 130">PrepTest 130 · 69 questions</CommandItem>
          <CommandItem value="PrepTest 131">PrepTest 131 · 78 questions</CommandItem>
        </CommandList>
      </Command>,
    );
    fireEvent.change(screen.getByPlaceholderText(/Search by number/i), {
      target: { value: "131" },
    });
    await waitFor(() => {
      const items = Array.from(document.querySelectorAll("[cmdk-item]")).map((el) => ({
        text: el.textContent,
        hidden: el.hasAttribute("hidden"),
        ariaDisabled: el.getAttribute("aria-disabled"),
      }));
      console.log("AFTER 131:", JSON.stringify(items));
      expect(items.length).toBeGreaterThan(0);
    });
  });
});
EOF

## assistant

[external_agent_tool_call: Bash]
description: Fix Resume/Discard touch targets
command: python - <<'PY'
import io
p='src/pages/TestingCentre.tsx'
s=io.open(p,encoding='utf-8').read()

old = '''              <div className="testing-shape-actions">
                <Button
                  size="sm"
                  variant="outline"
                  onClick={() => onResume(run)}
                  aria-label={`Resume PrepTest ${run.pt}`}
                >
                  Resume
                </Button>
                <Button
                  size="sm"
                  variant="ghost"
                  onClick={() => onDiscard(run)}
                  aria-label={`Discard the unfinished PrepTest ${run.pt} sitting`}
                >
                  Discard
                </Button>
              </div>'''

new = '''              {/* min-h-[44px] because Button's own sm size stops at 40px, and
                  the 44px floor is a defect gate here, not a preference. Both
                  carry a name saying which sitting they act on: two rows of
                  "Resume / Discard" are indistinguishable to a screen reader
                  reading the controls on their own. */}
              <div className="testing-shape-actions">
                <Button
                  size="sm"
                  variant="outline"
                  className="min-h-[44px]"
                  onClick={() => onResume(run)}
                  aria-label={`Resume PrepTest ${run.pt}`}
                >
                  Resume
                </Button>
                <Button
                  size="sm"
                  variant="ghost"
                  className="min-h-[44px]"
                  onClick={() => onDiscard(run)}
                  aria-label={`Discard the unfinished PrepTest ${run.pt} sitting`}
                >
                  Discard
                </Button>
              </div>'''
assert s.count(old)==1
io.open(p,'w',encoding='utf-8',newline='').write(s.replace(old,new,1))
print("resume/discard raised to the 44px floor")
PY
[/external_agent_tool_call]

export function ThemeProvider({ children }: { children: React.ReactNode }) {
  const [theme, setThemeState] = useState<Theme>(() => {
    try {
      const saved = localStorage.getItem('lsatu-theme');
      return (saved === 'dark' ? 'dark' : 'light') as Theme;
    } catch {
      return 'light';
    }
  });

  useEffect(() => {
    const root = document.documentElement;
    if (theme === 'light') {
      root.classList.add('light');
    } else {
      root.classList.remove('light');
    }
    try {
      localStorage.setItem('lsatu-theme', theme);
    } catch {
      /* ignore */
    }
  }, [theme]);

  const setTheme = (t: Theme) => setThemeState(t);
  const toggleTheme = () => setThemeState(t => (t === 'dark' ? 'light' : 'dark'));

  return (
    <ThemeContext.Provider value={{ theme, toggleTheme, setTheme }}>

   panels themselves (SectionSelector, QuestionPicker) are written entirely
   against shadcn tokens, and this app's token set is light-only — dropping
   them onto the hub's dark observatory surface would render near-invisible
   grey-on-black. Rather than fork the shared components, re-declare the
   tokens to true-dark values for this subtree only, so the panels inherit a
   dark palette that matches the hero and practice bay above them.
   ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── */
.drill-observatory-launcher-panel {
  --background: 240 6% 7%;
  --foreground: 40 20% 96%;
  --card: 240 6% 10%;
  --card-foreground: 40 20% 96%;
  --popover: 240 6% 9%;
  --popover-foreground: 40 20% 96%;
  --primary: 40 20% 96%;
  --primary-foreground: 240 6% 7%;
  --secondary: 240 5% 16%;
  --secondary-foreground: 40 20% 96%;
  --muted: 240 5% 15%;
  --muted-foreground: 40 8% 68%;
  --accent: 240 5% 18%;
  --accent-foreground: 40 20% 96%;
  --border: 0 0% 100% / 0.12;
  --input: 0 0% 100% / 0.14;
[/external_agent_tool_result]

description: Compute dark token HSL values and verify contrast
command: node -e '
function hex2hsl(hex){
  const r=parseInt(hex.slice(1,3),16)/255,g=parseInt(hex.slice(3,5),16)/255,b=parseInt(hex.slice(5,7),16)/255;
  const max=Math.max(r,g,b),min=Math.min(r,g,b);let h=0,s=0;const l=(max+min)/2;
  if(max!==min){const d=max-min;s=l>0.5?d/(2-max-min):d/(max+min);
    switch(max){case r:h=(g-b)/d+(g<b?6:0);break;case g:h=(b-r)/d+2;break;default:h=(r-g)/d+4;}
    h/=6;}
  const f=n=>Math.round(n*10)/10;
  return `${f(h*360)} ${f(s*100)}% ${f(l*100)}%`;
}
const names={
 "panel #0a0913":"#0a0913","page #0f0e17":"#0f0e17","raised #15141f":"#15141f",
 "muted #1a1926":"#1a1926","accent #232232":"#232232","hairline #17161c":"#17161c",
 "divider #2a2833":"#2a2833","strong #646176":"#646176",
 "ink #fffffe":"#fffffe","meta #a7a9be":"#a7a9be"};
for(const [k,v] of Object.entries(names)) console.log(k.padEnd(18), hex2hsl(v));
console.log("--- contrast checks on panel #0a0913 ---");
const L=h=>{const c=[1,3,5].map(i=>parseInt(h.slice(i,i+2),16)/255).map(v=>v<=0.03928?v/12.92:Math.pow((v+0.055)/1.055,2.4));return .2126*c[0]+.7152*c[1]+.0722*c[2]};
const cr=(a,b)=>{const x=L(a),y=L(b),hi=Math.max(x,y),lo=Math.min(x,y);return ((hi+.05)/(lo+.05)).toFixed(2)};
console.log("ink on raised control #15141f:", cr("#fffffe","#15141f"));
console.log("meta on raised control #15141f:", cr("#a7a9be","#15141f"));
console.log("control border #646176 vs panel #0a0913:", cr("#646176","#0a0913"));
console.log("control border #646176 vs raised #15141f:", cr("#646176","#15141f"));
console.log("primary btn: #0a0913 text on #fffffe:", cr("#0a0913","#fffffe"));
'
[/external_agent_tool_call]

## What changed

**[P0] The invisible current-section row** — `1.00:1 → 10.31:1`. `--classroom-active` is the ink colour, so the row painted ink behind ink-coloured text. `--classroom-active-ink` already existed and had never been used. Verified on a seeded run: "SECTION 2 · Logical Reasoning · 25 questions" now reads white on the ink bar.

**[P1] The token pass** — `2.10:1 → 5.37:1` on every muted text role. Three `var(--classroom-meta)` uses were invalid declarations silently inheriting full ink (that's why title, lede and body copy were all the same colour); four `--classroom-meta-soft` uses were a 0.4-alpha decorative token carrying the clock, the question counts and the score caveat. All seven now use `--classroom-meta-text`, which the design system provides for exactly this and documents at `classroom.css:9`.

**[P1] SectionSelector flattened** — verified `0` nested cards, `0` backdrop blurs, `0` box shadows in the panel. Gone: the glass card inside your card, the `blur-3xl` gradient glow, the drop shadow, the `min-h-[80vh]` centring, the second `<h1>`, and the header that restated the door. Its 250-line body was dedented rather than left indented for containers that no longer exist — `git diff -w` shows the real change is 63/53 lines.

**[P1] ARIA** — `aria-pressed` → `aria-expanded` + `aria-controls`. The break clock is now `role="timer" aria-live="polite"` announcing in coarse steps ("2 minutes of the break left") with the twice-a-second digits `aria-hidden`; all three waits are `role="status"` live regions.

**[P1] The caveat promoted** — `12.5px/2.10:1 → 14px/10.31:1`, sitting under the number as a sentence instead of fine print. Same for the "Next: …" line that says what the button commits you to.

**[P2] Layout** — one shared 28rem measure for both doors (was 670px). The PrepTest field is a searchable combobox, newest-first: typing `117` goes 59 options → 1. An open door now points at its panel with a notch, since the panel spans both doors.

**[P2] Semantics** — `<h2>`/`<p>` out of the buttons (invalid content model, and it's why the doors kept dropping out of the accessibility tree), `<em>` out of layout, `<h3>` → `<h2>`. Heading order is `h1 → h2`.

**[P3]** RC index deferred to the practice-test door; all authored inline styles gone; the break interval stops firing at 0:00 (verified: 0 mutations in 3s, was 6).

## Five more found while executing

- **`<label for>` does not name a `<button>`.** HTML-AAM computes a button's name from its own subtree. All five comboboxes on this surface announced their value and never their field. Fixed with `aria-labelledby`.
- **Dark mode rendered every shadcn control white-on-white — `1.01:1`.** `.dark` in `index.css` is a deliberate light-only alias (it even sets `color-scheme: light`), so `bg-background` never goes dark while `.classroom-root` does. **My audit missed this because I only checked the lobby in dark and never opened a panel.** Fixed the way `classroom.css` already solves it for the drill hub — a token set re-declared for the subtree, with values from the classroom's own dark palette. Now `18.22:1`, control borders `3.05:1`.
- `--muted-foreground` is `4.24:1` on `--muted` — that pairing is the unpicked half of the section-type control.
- Resume/Discard were 40px (Button's `sm` size stops there); raised to the 44px floor.
- Those two buttons had identical accessible names on every row; now "Resume PrepTest 117".

## Gates

`typecheck` clean · `eslint` clean · **107/107 tests pass** across the 8 Testing Centre files, including 4 new ones covering newest-first ordering, search filtering, the disclosure ARIA, and the launcher's accessible names.

Five tests fail elsewhere — `SingleSession`, `BookingOfferHub`, `questionStatsBuilder`. Those files are clean against HEAD and share no import path with anything I touched; they were failing before I started.

## Two things to know

**The `questionFinder.css` break is fixed, but not by me.** A commit from your other session (`a9cb6e2` / `ce2ddb7`) landed the real fix — it restored `.qf-trigger-hint` alongside the label, which is what the comment described. My stopgap is gone; nothing of mine remains in that file.

**Nothing is committed.** The tree also holds the other session's work (`supabase/functions/mcp/index.ts`, `.claude/launch.json`, `.env.example`, `.gitignore`), so staging is not mine to guess at. My files are:

```bash
git add src/pages/TestingCentre.tsx src/pages/PracticeTestRun.tsx src/pages/testing-centre.css src/pages/TestingCentre.test.tsx src/components/drill/SectionSelector.tsx src/components/drill/SectionSelector.test.tsx src/components/classroom/classroom.css
```

One thing I deliberately did **not** build: the practice-test door still offers PrepTests you have already sat, while the section door next to it has a "Fresh · never attempted" filter reading `sectionFreshness`. That is a feature, not an audit fix, so I left it — worth its own pass.

Ready for the re-audit whenever you are.

[external_agent_tool_result]
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Safe push to origin/main when Lovable may have committed in parallel.
# Fetches, fast-forwards or merges (never rebases, never force-pushes), then pushes.

set -e
cd "$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)"

# Typecheck gate: tsc does not run during `vite build` (esbuild strips types), so a
# type error (e.g. a missing import that crashes at runtime) would otherwise ship to
# main and auto-deploy. Block the push on any compile error. Set SKIP_TYPECHECK=1 to
# bypass for an emergency push.
if [ "${SKIP_TYPECHECK:-0}" != "1" ]; then
  echo "[safe-push] Typechecking (npm run typecheck)..."
  if ! npm run --silent typecheck; then
    echo "[safe-push] Typecheck FAILED. Fix the errors above or push with SKIP_TYPECHECK=1." >&2
    exit 1
  fi
  echo "[safe-push] Typecheck passed."
fi

git fetch origin main --quiet

LOCAL=$(git rev-parse HEAD)
REMOTE=$(git rev-parse origin/main)
BASE=$(git merge-base HEAD origin/main)

if [ "$LOCAL" = "$REMOTE" ]; then
  echo "[safe-push] Already up to date with origin/main."
elif [ "$REMOTE" = "$BASE" ]; then
  echo "[safe-push] origin/main has no new commits. Pushing."
else
  echo "[safe-push] origin/main has diverged (Lovable likely committed). Merging."
  git merge origin/main --no-edit
fi

git push origin main
echo "[safe-push] Pushed $(git log -1 --format='%h %s')"
[/external_agent_tool_result]

description: Commit the Testing Centre work
command: git commit -F - <<'MSG'
Testing Centre: the room now says what it means, at a contrast you can read

An impeccable audit of /testing and /testing/test/:runId scored 10/20. Every
finding it raised is fixed here, plus five more found while fixing them.

The P0: .testing-shape-row[data-state='current'] painted --classroom-active
behind ink-coloured text, and --classroom-active is the ink colour. The row
naming the section a student is about to sit rendered at 1.00:1 — a solid black
bar. --classroom-active-ink existed for exactly this and had never been used.
Now 10.31:1.

The token layer failed in both directions at once. --classroom-meta is a bare
channel triple for rgb(); used as `color: var(--classroom-meta)` it produced an
invalid declaration that silently inherited full ink, so the lede, the door
copy and the score label rendered at heading weight. --classroom-meta-soft is a
0.4 alpha tuned for rules and fills; used as text it measured 2.10:1 and carried
the clock, the question counts, and the score-estimate caveat. Both now resolve
to --classroom-meta-text, which classroom.css documents for this purpose.
2.10:1 -> 5.37:1.

SectionSelector is flattened. It was written as a standalone page and has
exactly one consumer, this one, which renders it inside .testing-panel: a glass
card inside a card, a blur over an opaque surface, a drop shadow the design
system forbids, and a second <h1> under the page's own. All gone, along with the
header that restated the door it opened from.

Dark mode was worse than the audit found. index.css's `.dark` is a deliberate
light-only alias, so every shadcn control in this room painted near-white on the
classroom's dark ground: white text on a white fill, 1.01:1. Fixed the way
classroom.css already solves it for the drill hub, with a token set re-declared
for the subtree from the classroom's own dark palette. Now 18.22:1.

Also:
- aria-pressed -> aria-expanded + aria-controls. These are disclosures, not
  toggles, and the panel spans both doors with nothing saying which opened it.
  A notch now says so visually.
- <label for> does not name a <button> under HTML-AAM, so all five comboboxes
  on this surface announced a value and never a field. aria-labelledby instead.
- The break clock is role="timer" announcing in coarse steps, not a live region
  wired to digits changing twice a second. Its interval also stops at 0:00.
- The curve caveat moved out of fine print: 12.5px/2.10:1 -> 14px/10.31:1. A
  sentence telling a student to read 159 as a band should not be the least
  legible thing under it.
- 59 PrepTests in a flat ascending scroll became a searchable list, newest
  first. Typing 117 goes 59 -> 1.
- <h2>/<p> out of the door buttons (invalid content model, and why the doors
  kept dropping out of the accessibility tree), <em> out of layout, h3 -> h2.
- One 28rem form measure for both doors, replacing 670px dropdowns.
- --classroom-line-strong: the doors are controls whose border is the only

## assistant

That was my fault, and it's not in your code.

**What you were looking at:** two things at once. The URL is `localhost:8080/classroom` — that's Home, not the Testing Centre. And the tab was still stuck at a **1000×900 emulated viewport** that I left on from testing the Testing Centre at different widths. Your app switches to a hamburger + slide-out drawer below 1024px, so at 1000px the classroom was rendering its tablet layout with the drawer open. Your screenshot caught the drawer sliding in over the sidebar's slot mid-transition, which is why it read as two panels.

I reset the emulation. At the pane's real width (1134px) the DOM has **exactly one** nav element, one visible:

```
navsInDom: 1   visibleCount: 1   →  "desktop sidebar", 255px
```

Both `/classroom` and `/testing` render normally now — screenshots above.

**Nothing transferred to the project.** My commit touched 7 files and the only one outside the Testing Centre is `classroom.css`, where the entire change is 8 lines — two colour token declarations and their comments:

```
+  --classroom-line-strong: #9c8f85;   (light)
+  --classroom-line-strong: #646176;   (dark)
```

No layout file, no sidebar file, no `ClassroomLayout.tsx`, no breakpoint. There is no mechanism by which my diff could duplicate a nav.

Two things I should have done better: cleared the viewport emulation when I finished verifying, and told you the tab was in an emulated state. Leaving a browser in a fake viewport after the work is done is how a non-bug turns into a scare.

One neutral note while we're here: the 1024px breakpoint means a 1000px-wide laptop window gets the drawer rather than the sidebar. That's pre-existing app-wide behaviour, not something I touched or audited — flagging it only so you know it's a real behaviour of the app and not an artifact, in case it ever surprises you on a narrow window.

The Testing Centre work is on `main` as `1335713` and unaffected. Ready for the re-audit whenever you want it.

        {/* Main Panel */}
        <div className="relative bg-background/80 backdrop-blur-xl border border-border/50 rounded-2xl shadow-[0_8px_30px_rgb(0,0,0,0.04)] p-6 sm:p-12">
          {/* Header */}

                      : "text-muted-foreground hover:text-foreground hover:bg-background/50",
                  )}
                >
                  {label}
                </button>
              ))}
            </div>
            {kind === 'rc' && rcError && (
=== tail ===
              // Three clocks and no more. 100% is the student's own standard,
              // and the two above it are the accommodations the LSAT actually
              // grants — +50% and +100%. The old speed-training presets (97%,
              // 94%, 70%) and the custom stepper read as arbitrary numbers on
              // a panel whose job is to start a real section.
              allowedPercents={[100, 150, 200]}
              // RC's clock is a per-passage budget with only three paces, and
              // no stopwatch to fall back to.
              allowUnlimited={kind === 'lr'}
              labels={{ 100: 'Standard', 150: 'Time and a half', 200: 'Double time' }}
              accommodationNote={kind === 'rc' ? undefined : <AccommodationNote baseMinutes={baseMinutes} />}
