# ---------- 1. BreakScreen: real timer semantics, and stop ticking at zero ----------
old = '''/** The break, on its own clock. Skippable — it is a break, not a gate. */
function BreakScreen({ run, onDone }: { run: Run; onDone: () => void }) {
  const startedAt = run.breakStartedAt ? Date.parse(run.breakStartedAt) : Date.now();
  const [remaining, setRemaining] = React.useState(() =>
    Math.max(0, PRACTICE_TEST_BREAK_SECONDS - (Date.now() - startedAt) / 1000),
  );

  React.useEffect(() => {
    const id = window.setInterval(() => {
      setRemaining(Math.max(0, PRACTICE_TEST_BREAK_SECONDS - (Date.now() - startedAt) / 1000));
    }, 500);
    return () => window.clearInterval(id);
  }, [startedAt]);

  return (
    <div className="testing-panel">
      <div className="testing-score">
        <strong>{mmss(remaining)}</strong>
        <span>break remaining</span>
      </div>
      <p className="testing-note">
        Stand up, get water, look at something further away than a screen. The
        real test gives you this, and taking it is part of practising the
        sitting rather than just the questions.
      </p>
      <div className="testing-actions">
        <Button onClick={onDone}>
          {remaining > 0 ? "Skip the break and continue" : "Continue"}
        </Button>
      </div>
    </div>
  );
}'''

new = '''/**
 * What a ticking clock says out loud.
 *
 * The digits change twice a second, and a live region wired straight to them
 * would talk over itself continuously for ten minutes. Coarse steps instead:
 * whole minutes while there are minutes left, then ten-second steps in the last
 * one. The string only changes when the step does, so the region only speaks
 * when there is something new to say.
 */
function breakAnnouncement(remaining: number): string {
  const seconds = Math.ceil(remaining);
  if (seconds <= 0) return "The break is over. Continue when you are ready.";
  if (seconds <= 60) return `${Math.ceil(seconds / 10) * 10} seconds of the break left.`;
  const minutes = Math.ceil(seconds / 60);
  return `${minutes} ${minutes === 1 ? "minute" : "minutes"} of the break left.`;
}

/** The break, on its own clock. Skippable — it is a break, not a gate. */
function BreakScreen({ run, onDone }: { run: Run; onDone: () => void }) {
  const startedAt = run.breakStartedAt ? Date.parse(run.breakStartedAt) : Date.now();
  const [remaining, setRemaining] = React.useState(() =>
    Math.max(0, PRACTICE_TEST_BREAK_SECONDS - (Date.now() - startedAt) / 1000),
  );

  React.useEffect(() => {
    if (remaining <= 0) return;
    const id = window.setInterval(() => {
      setRemaining(Math.max(0, PRACTICE_TEST_BREAK_SECONDS - (Date.now() - startedAt) / 1000));
    }, 500);
    return () => window.clearInterval(id);
  }, [startedAt, remaining]);

  return (
    <div className="testing-panel">
      <h2 className="testing-heading">Break</h2>
      <div className="testing-score">
        {/* The digits are for the eye. Assistive tech is given the coarse
            announcement below instead of a number that changes 1,200 times. */}
        <strong aria-hidden>{mmss(remaining)}</strong>
        <span aria-hidden>break remaining</span>
        <span className="sr-only" role="timer" aria-live="polite">
          {breakAnnouncement(remaining)}
        </span>
      </div>
      <p className="testing-note">
        Stand up, get water, look at something further away than a screen. The
        real test gives you this, and taking it is part of practising the
        sitting rather than just the questions.
      </p>
      <div className="testing-actions">
        <Button onClick={onDone}>
          {remaining > 0 ? "Skip the break and continue" : "Continue"}
        </Button>
      </div>
    </div>
  );
}'''
assert s.count(old) == 1, "BreakScreen anchor"
s = s.replace(old, new, 1)

'''

description: Wire SectionSelector accessible names
command: python - <<'PY'
import io, re
p = 'src/components/drill/SectionSelector.tsx'
s = io.open(p, encoding='utf-8').read()

# Give every label an id, at whatever indentation it already sits on.
s, n1 = re.subn(
    r'(\n(\s+))htmlFor="([a-z]+-select)"',
    lambda m: f'{m.group(1)}htmlFor="{m.group(3)}"{m.group(1)}id="{m.group(3)}-label"',
    s,
)

# Point each trigger at its label. <label for> does not name a <button> under
# HTML-AAM, so these comboboxes announced their value and never their field.
# The self-reference keeps the value in the announcement.
s, n2 = re.subn(
    r'(\n(\s+))className="text-sm"(\n\s+)id="([a-z]+-select)"',
    lambda m: (f'{m.group(1)}aria-labelledby="{m.group(4)}-label {m.group(4)}"'
               f'{m.group(1)}className="text-sm"{m.group(3)}id="{m.group(4)}"'),
    s,
)

assert n1 == 3, n1
assert n2 == 3, n2
io.open(p, 'w', encoding='utf-8', newline='').write(s)
print(f'SectionSelector: {n1} label ids, {n2} trigger names')
PY
sed -n '353,372p' src/components/drill/SectionSelector.tsx

import { fireEvent, render, screen, waitFor } from "@testing-library/react";
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import {
  Command,
  CommandEmpty,
  CommandInput,
  CommandItem,
  CommandList,
} from "@/components/ui/command";

describe("cmdk in jsdom", () => {
  it("filters", async () => {
    render(
      <Command>
        <CommandInput placeholder="Search by number" />
        <CommandList>
          <CommandEmpty>none</CommandEmpty>
          <CommandItem value="PrepTest 130">PrepTest 130 · 69 questions</CommandItem>
          <CommandItem value="PrepTest 131">PrepTest 131 · 78 questions</CommandItem>
        </CommandList>
      </Command>,
    );
    fireEvent.change(screen.getByPlaceholderText(/Search by number/i), {
      target: { value: "131" },
    });
    await waitFor(() => {
      const items = Array.from(document.querySelectorAll("[cmdk-item]")).map((el) => ({
        text: el.textContent,
        hidden: el.getAttribute("hidden"),
        ariaHidden: el.getAttribute("aria-hidden"),
        display: (el as HTMLElement).style.display,
      }));
      console.log(JSON.stringify(items, null, 1));
      expect(items.length).toBeGreaterThan(0);
    });
  });
});
EOF

import { fireEvent, render, screen, waitFor } from "@testing-library/react";
import { beforeAll, describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import {
  Command,
  CommandEmpty,
  CommandInput,
  CommandItem,
  CommandList,
} from "@/components/ui/command";

beforeAll(() => {
  const proto = window.HTMLElement.prototype as unknown as Record<string, unknown>;
  proto.scrollIntoView = () => undefined;
});

describe("cmdk in jsdom", () => {
  it("filters", async () => {
    render(
      <Command>
        <CommandInput placeholder="Search by number" />
        <CommandList>
          <CommandEmpty>none</CommandEmpty>
          <CommandItem value="PrepTest 130">PrepTest 130 · 69 questions</CommandItem>
          <CommandItem value="PrepTest 131">PrepTest 131 · 78 questions</CommandItem>
        </CommandList>
      </Command>,
    );
    fireEvent.change(screen.getByPlaceholderText(/Search by number/i), {
      target: { value: "131" },
    });
    await waitFor(() => {
      const items = Array.from(document.querySelectorAll("[cmdk-item]")).map((el) => ({
        text: el.textContent,
        hidden: el.hasAttribute("hidden"),
        ariaDisabled: el.getAttribute("aria-disabled"),
      }));
      console.log("AFTER 131:", JSON.stringify(items));
      expect(items.length).toBeGreaterThan(0);
    });
  });
});
EOF

## assistant

[external_agent_tool_call: Bash]
description: Fix Resume/Discard touch targets
command: python - <<'PY'
import io
p='src/pages/TestingCentre.tsx'
s=io.open(p,encoding='utf-8').read()

old = '''              <div className="testing-shape-actions">
                <Button
                  size="sm"
                  variant="outline"
                  onClick={() => onResume(run)}
                  aria-label={`Resume PrepTest ${run.pt}`}
                >
                  Resume
                </Button>
                <Button
                  size="sm"
                  variant="ghost"
                  onClick={() => onDiscard(run)}
                  aria-label={`Discard the unfinished PrepTest ${run.pt} sitting`}
                >
                  Discard
                </Button>
              </div>'''

new = '''              {/* min-h-[44px] because Button's own sm size stops at 40px, and
                  the 44px floor is a defect gate here, not a preference. Both
                  carry a name saying which sitting they act on: two rows of
                  "Resume / Discard" are indistinguishable to a screen reader
                  reading the controls on their own. */}
              <div className="testing-shape-actions">
                <Button
                  size="sm"
                  variant="outline"
                  className="min-h-[44px]"
                  onClick={() => onResume(run)}
                  aria-label={`Resume PrepTest ${run.pt}`}
                >
                  Resume
                </Button>
                <Button
                  size="sm"
                  variant="ghost"
                  className="min-h-[44px]"
                  onClick={() => onDiscard(run)}
                  aria-label={`Discard the unfinished PrepTest ${run.pt} sitting`}
                >
                  Discard
                </Button>
              </div>'''
assert s.count(old)==1
io.open(p,'w',encoding='utf-8',newline='').write(s.replace(old,new,1))
print("resume/discard raised to the 44px floor")
PY
[/external_agent_tool_call]

export function ThemeProvider({ children }: { children: React.ReactNode }) {
  const [theme, setThemeState] = useState<Theme>(() => {
    try {
      const saved = localStorage.getItem('lsatu-theme');
      return (saved === 'dark' ? 'dark' : 'light') as Theme;
    } catch {
      return 'light';
    }
  });

  useEffect(() => {
    const root = document.documentElement;
    if (theme === 'light') {
      root.classList.add('light');
    } else {
      root.classList.remove('light');
    }
    try {
      localStorage.setItem('lsatu-theme', theme);
    } catch {
      /* ignore */
    }
  }, [theme]);

  const setTheme = (t: Theme) => setThemeState(t);
  const toggleTheme = () => setThemeState(t => (t === 'dark' ? 'light' : 'dark'));

  return (
    <ThemeContext.Provider value={{ theme, toggleTheme, setTheme }}>

command: node -e '
function hex2hsl(hex){
  const r=parseInt(hex.slice(1,3),16)/255,g=parseInt(hex.slice(3,5),16)/255,b=parseInt(hex.slice(5,7),16)/255;
  const max=Math.max(r,g,b),min=Math.min(r,g,b);let h=0,s=0;const l=(max+min)/2;
  if(max!==min){const d=max-min;s=l>0.5?d/(2-max-min):d/(max+min);
    switch(max){case r:h=(g-b)/d+(g<b?6:0);break;case g:h=(b-r)/d+2;break;default:h=(r-g)/d+4;}
    h/=6;}
  const f=n=>Math.round(n*10)/10;
  return `${f(h*360)} ${f(s*100)}% ${f(l*100)}%`;
}
const names={
 "panel #0a0913":"#0a0913","page #0f0e17":"#0f0e17","raised #15141f":"#15141f",
 "muted #1a1926":"#1a1926","accent #232232":"#232232","hairline #17161c":"#17161c",
 "divider #2a2833":"#2a2833","strong #646176":"#646176",
 "ink #fffffe":"#fffffe","meta #a7a9be":"#a7a9be"};
for(const [k,v] of Object.entries(names)) console.log(k.padEnd(18), hex2hsl(v));
console.log("--- contrast checks on panel #0a0913 ---");
const L=h=>{const c=[1,3,5].map(i=>parseInt(h.slice(i,i+2),16)/255).map(v=>v<=0.03928?v/12.92:Math.pow((v+0.055)/1.055,2.4));return .2126*c[0]+.7152*c[1]+.0722*c[2]};
const cr=(a,b)=>{const x=L(a),y=L(b),hi=Math.max(x,y),lo=Math.min(x,y);return ((hi+.05)/(lo+.05)).toFixed(2)};
console.log("ink on raised control #15141f:", cr("#fffffe","#15141f"));
console.log("meta on raised control #15141f:", cr("#a7a9be","#15141f"));
console.log("control border #646176 vs panel #0a0913:", cr("#646176","#0a0913"));
console.log("control border #646176 vs raised #15141f:", cr("#646176","#15141f"));
console.log("primary btn: #0a0913 text on #fffffe:", cr("#0a0913","#fffffe"));

## assistant

[external_agent_tool_call: Bash]
description: Compute the light muted-foreground fix
command: node -e '
function hex2hsl(hex){const r=parseInt(hex.slice(1,3),16)/255,g=parseInt(hex.slice(3,5),16)/255,b=parseInt(hex.slice(5,7),16)/255;const max=Math.max(r,g,b),min=Math.min(r,g,b);let h=0,s=0;const l=(max+min)/2;if(max!==min){const d=max-min;s=l>0.5?d/(2-max-min):d/(max+min);switch(max){case r:h=(g-b)/d+(g<b?6:0);break;case g:h=(b-r)/d+2;break;default:h=(r-g)/d+4;}h/=6;}const f=n=>Math.round(n*10)/10;return `${f(h*360)} ${f(s*100)}% ${f(l*100)}%`;}
const L=h=>{const c=[1,3,5].map(i=>parseInt(h.slice(i,i+2),16)/255).map(v=>v<=0.03928?v/12.92:Math.pow((v+0.055)/1.055,2.4));return .2126*c[0]+.7152*c[1]+.0722*c[2]};
const cr=(a,b)=>{const x=L(a),y=L(b),hi=Math.max(x,y),lo=Math.min(x,y);return ((hi+.05)/(lo+.05)).toFixed(2)};
console.log("#6d6a67 ->", hex2hsl("#6d6a67"));
console.log("#6d6a67 on muted track #f2f2f2:", cr("#6d6a67","#f2f2f2"));
console.log("#6d6a67 on white:", cr("#6d6a67","#ffffff"));
console.log("current #737373 on #f2f2f2:", cr("#737373","#f2f2f2"));
'
[/external_agent_tool_call]

## assistant

All nine executed and verified in the browser. Scope stayed on the Testing Centre.

## What changed

**[P0] The invisible current-section row** — `1.00:1 → 10.31:1`. `--classroom-active` is the ink colour, so the row painted ink behind ink-coloured text. `--classroom-active-ink` already existed and had never been used. Verified on a seeded run: "SECTION 2 · Logical Reasoning · 25 questions" now reads white on the ink bar.

**[P1] The token pass** — `2.10:1 → 5.37:1` on every muted text role. Three `var(--classroom-meta)` uses were invalid declarations silently inheriting full ink (that's why title, lede and body copy were all the same colour); four `--classroom-meta-soft` uses were a 0.4-alpha decorative token carrying the clock, the question counts and the score caveat. All seven now use `--classroom-meta-text`, which the design system provides for exactly this and documents at `classroom.css:9`.

**[P1] SectionSelector flattened** — verified `0` nested cards, `0` backdrop blurs, `0` box shadows in the panel. Gone: the glass card inside your card, the `blur-3xl` gradient glow, the drop shadow, the `min-h-[80vh]` centring, the second `<h1>`, and the header that restated the door. Its 250-line body was dedented rather than left indented for containers that no longer exist — `git diff -w` shows the real change is 63/53 lines.

**[P1] ARIA** — `aria-pressed` → `aria-expanded` + `aria-controls`. The break clock is now `role="timer" aria-live="polite"` announcing in coarse steps ("2 minutes of the break left") with the twice-a-second digits `aria-hidden`; all three waits are `role="status"` live regions.

**[P1] The caveat promoted** — `12.5px/2.10:1 → 14px/10.31:1`, sitting under the number as a sentence instead of fine print. Same for the "Next: …" line that says what the button commits you to.

**[P2] Layout** — one shared 28rem measure for both doors (was 670px). The PrepTest field is a searchable combobox, newest-first: typing `117` goes 59 options → 1. An open door now points at its panel with a notch, since the panel spans both doors.

**[P2] Semantics** — `<h2>`/`<p>` out of the buttons (invalid content model, and it's why the doors kept dropping out of the accessibility tree), `<em>` out of layout, `<h3>` → `<h2>`. Heading order is `h1 → h2`.

**[P3]** RC index deferred to the practice-test door; all authored inline styles gone; the break interval stops firing at 0:00 (verified: 0 mutations in 3s, was 6).

## Five more found while executing

- **`<label for>` does not name a `<button>`.** HTML-AAM computes a button's name from its own subtree. All five comboboxes on this surface announced their value and never their field. Fixed with `aria-labelledby`.
- **Dark mode rendered every shadcn control white-on-white — `1.01:1`.** `.dark` in `index.css` is a deliberate light-only alias (it even sets `color-scheme: light`), so `bg-background` never goes dark while `.classroom-root` does. **My audit missed this because I only checked the lobby in dark and never opened a panel.** Fixed the way `classroom.css` already solves it for the drill hub — a token set re-declared for the subtree, with values from the classroom's own dark palette. Now `18.22:1`, control borders `3.05:1`.
- `--muted-foreground` is `4.24:1` on `--muted` — that pairing is the unpicked half of the section-type control.
- Resume/Discard were 40px (Button's `sm` size stops there); raised to the 44px floor.
- Those two buttons had identical accessible names on every row; now "Resume PrepTest 117".

## Gates

`typecheck` clean · `eslint` clean · **107/107 tests pass** across the 8 Testing Centre files, including 4 new ones covering newest-first ordering, search filtering, the disclosure ARIA, and the launcher's accessible names.

Five tests fail elsewhere — `SingleSession`, `BookingOfferHub`, `questionStatsBuilder`. Those files are clean against HEAD and share no import path with anything I touched; they were failing before I started.

## Two things to know

**The `questionFinder.css` break is fixed, but not by me.** A commit from your other session (`a9cb6e2` / `ce2ddb7`) landed the real fix — it restored `.qf-trigger-hint` alongside the label, which is what the comment described. My stopgap is gone; nothing of mine remains in that file.

**Nothing is committed.** The tree also holds the other session's work (`supabase/functions/mcp/index.ts`, `.claude/launch.json`, `.env.example`, `.gitignore`), so staging is not mine to guess at. My files are:

```bash
git add src/pages/TestingCentre.tsx src/pages/PracticeTestRun.tsx src/pages/testing-centre.css src/pages/TestingCentre.test.tsx src/components/drill/SectionSelector.tsx src/components/drill/SectionSelector.test.tsx src/components/classroom/classroom.css
```

One thing I deliberately did **not** build: the practice-test door still offers PrepTests you have already sat, while the section door next to it has a "Fresh · never attempted" filter reading `sectionFreshness`. That is a feature, not an audit fix, so I left it — worth its own pass.

Ready for the re-audit whenever you are.

light-only alias, so every shadcn control in this room painted near-white on the
classroom's dark ground: white text on a white fill, 1.01:1. Fixed the way

Run systematic **technical** quality checks and generate a comprehensive report. Don't fix issues — document them for other commands to address.

This is a code-level audit, not a design critique. Check what's measurable and verifiable in the implementation.

## Diagnostic Scan

Run comprehensive checks across 5 dimensions. Score each dimension 0-4 using the criteria below.

### 1. Accessibility (A11y)

**Check for**:
- **Contrast issues**: Text contrast ratios < 4.5:1 (or 7:1 for AAA)
- **Missing ARIA**: Interactive elements without proper roles, labels, or states
- **Keyboard navigation**: Missing focus indicators, illogical tab order, keyboard traps
- **Semantic HTML**: Improper heading hierarchy, missing landmarks, divs instead of buttons
- **Alt text**: Missing or poor image descriptions
- **Form issues**: Inputs without labels, poor error messaging, missing required indicators

**Score 0-4**: 0=Inaccessible (fails WCAG A), 1=Major gaps (few ARIA labels, no keyboard nav), 2=Partial (some a11y effort, significant gaps), 3=Good (WCAG AA mostly met, minor gaps), 4=Excellent (WCAG AA fully met, approaches AAA)

### 2. Performance

**Check for**:
- **Layout thrashing**: Reading/writing layout properties in loops
- **Expensive animations**: Casual layout-property animation, unbounded blur/filter/shadow effects, or effects that visibly drop frames
- **Missing optimization**: Images without lazy loading, unoptimized assets, missing will-change
- **Bundle size**: Unnecessary imports, unused dependencies
- **Render performance**: Unnecessary re-renders, missing memoization

**Score 0-4**: 0=Severe issues (layout thrash, unoptimized everything), 1=Major problems (no lazy loading, expensive animations), 2=Partial (some optimization, gaps remain), 3=Good (mostly optimized, minor improvements possible), 4=Excellent (fast, lean, well-optimized)

### 3. Theming

**Check for**:
- **Hard-coded colors**: Colors not using design tokens
- **Broken dark mode**: Missing dark mode variants, poor contrast in dark theme
- **Inconsistent tokens**: Using wrong tokens, mixing token types
- **Theme switching issues**: Values that don't update on theme change

**Score 0-4**: 0=No theming (hard-coded everything), 1=Minimal tokens (mostly hard-coded), 2=Partial (tokens exist but inconsistently used), 3=Good (tokens used, minor hard-coded values), 4=Excellent (full token system, dark mode works perfectly)

### 4. Responsive Design

**Check for**:
- **Fixed widths**: Hard-coded widths that break on mobile
- **Touch targets**: Interactive elements < 44x44px
- **Horizontal scroll**: Content overflow on narrow viewports
- **Text scaling**: Layouts that break when text size increases
- **Missing breakpoints**: No mobile/tablet variants

**Score 0-4**: 0=Desktop-only (breaks on mobile), 1=Major issues (some breakpoints, many failures), 2=Partial (works on mobile, rough edges), 3=Good (responsive, minor touch target or overflow issues), 4=Excellent (fluid, all viewports, proper touch targets)

### 5. Anti-Patterns (CRITICAL)

Check against ALL the **DON'T** guidelines from the parent impeccable skill (already loaded in this context). Look for AI slop tells (AI color palette, gradient text, glassmorphism, hero metrics, card grids, generic fonts) and general design anti-patterns (gray on color, nested cards, bounce easing, redundant copy).

**Score 0-4**: 0=AI slop gallery (5+ tells), 1=Heavy AI aesthetic (3-4 tells), 2=Some tells (1-2 noticeable), 3=Mostly clean (subtle issues only), 4=No AI tells (distinctive, intentional design)

## Generate Report

window.__aud = (() => {
  const lin = c => { c/=255; return c<=0.03928 ? c/12.92 : Math.pow((c+0.055)/1.055,2.4); };
  const L = rgb => 0.2126*lin(rgb[0])+0.7152*lin(rgb[1])+0.0722*lin(rgb[2]);
  const parse = s => { const m=(s||'').match(/[\d.]+/g); return m?[+m[0],+m[1],+m[2],m[3]!==undefined?+m[3]:1]:null; };
  const over = (fg,bg) => { const a=fg[3]; return [0,1,2].map(i=>fg[i]*a+bg[i]*(1-a)); };
  function bgOf(el){
    let n=el, acc=null;
    while(n && n.nodeType===1){
      const c=parse(getComputedStyle(n).backgroundColor);
      if(c && c[3]>0){ acc = acc? over(acc.concat?acc:acc, c) : c; if(c[3]>=1) return acc.length===4?over(acc,[255,255,255]):acc; }
      n=n.parentElement;
    }
    return [255,255,255];
  }
  function ratio(a,b){ const l1=L(a),l2=L(b); return (Math.max(l1,l2)+0.05)/(Math.min(l1,l2)+0.05); }
  function contrast(el){
    const cs=getComputedStyle(el);
    let fg=parse(cs.color); const bg=bgOf(el);
    if(!fg) return null;
    if(fg[3]<1) fg=over(fg,bg);
    return { r:+ratio(fg.slice(0,3),bg.slice(0,3)).toFixed(2), fg:cs.color, bg:`rgb(${bg.slice(0,3).map(Math.round)})`, size:parseFloat(cs.fontSize), weight:cs.fontWeight };
  }
  const vis = el => { const r=el.getBoundingClientRect(); const cs=getComputedStyle(el); return r.width>0&&r.height>0&&cs.visibility!=='hidden'&&cs.display!=='none'&&cs.opacity!=='0'; };
  return {
    contrastFails(root){
      root = root||document.body;
      const out=[];
      root.querySelectorAll('*').forEach(el=>{
        if(!vis(el)) return;
        const txt=[...el.childNodes].filter(n=>n.nodeType===3&&n.textContent.trim()).map(n=>n.textContent.trim()).join(' ');
        if(!txt) return;
        const c=contrast(el); if(!c) return;
        const large = c.size>=24 || (c.size>=18.66 && +c.weight>=700);
        const need = large?3:4.5;
        if(c.r < need) out.push({txt:txt.slice(0,60), tag:el.tagName+'.'+(el.className&&el.className.baseVal!==undefined?el.className.baseVal:el.className||'').toString().split(' ').slice(0,2).join('.'), ...c, need});
      });
      return out;
    },
    targets(root){
      root=root||document.body;
      const out=[];
      root.querySelectorAll('button,a[href],input,select,textarea,[role=button],[role=combobox],[role=tab],[tabindex]:not([tabindex="-1"])').forEach(el=>{
        if(!vis(el)) return;
        const r=el.getBoundingClientRect();
        if(r.width<44||r.height<44) out.push({tag:el.tagName, label:(el.getAttribute('aria-label')||el.textContent||'').trim().slice(0,40), w:+r.width.toFixed(1), h:+r.height.toFixed(1), cls:(el.className||'').toString().slice(0,60)});
      });
      return out;
    },
    names(root){
      root=root||document.body;
      const out=[];
      root.querySelectorAll('button,a[href],input,select,textarea,[role=combobox],[role=button]').forEach(el=>{
        if(!vis(el)) return;
        let n=el.getAttribute('aria-label')||'';
        const lb=el.getAttribute('aria-labelledby');
        if(!n&&lb) n=lb.split(/\s+/).map(id=>{const t=document.getElementById(id);return t?(t===el?(t.textContent||t.value||''):t.textContent):'';}).join(' ').trim();
        if(!n) n=(el.textContent||'').trim();
        if(!n&&el.id) { const l=document.querySelector(`label[for="${el.id}"]`); if(l&&el.tagName!=='BUTTON') n=l.textContent.trim(); }
        if(!n) n=el.title||el.getAttribute('placeholder')||'';
        if(!n.trim()) out.push({tag:el.tagName, role:el.getAttribute('role'), id:el.id, cls:(el.className||'').toString().slice(0,60)});
      });
      return out;
    },
    overflow(){ return { scrollW: document.documentElement.scrollWidth, clientW: document.documentElement.clientWidth, overflows: document.documentElement.scrollWidth > document.documentElement.clientWidth+1 }; },
    headings(){ return [...document.querySelectorAll('h1,h2,h3,h4,h5,h6')].filter(vis).map(h=>h.tagName+': '+h.textContent.trim().slice(0,60)); },
    blurs(){ return [...document.querySelectorAll('*')].filter(el=>vis(el)&&/blur/.test(getComputedStyle(el).backdropFilter+getComputedStyle(el).filter)).map(el=>({tag:el.tagName,cls:(el.className||'').toString().slice(0,60),f:getComputedStyle(el).backdropFilter,fl:getComputedStyle(el).filter})); },
    ratio, contrast, bgOf, parse
  };
})(); 'probe ready'

/** The panel both doors open into. Named so a door can point at it. */
const PANEL_ID = "testing-door-panel";

/**
 * RC sections, loaded only once something needs them.
 *
 * This used to run on mount, which pulled the whole Reading Comprehension index
 * across the wire to render two buttons. Only the practice-test door needs it —
 * the section door asks SectionSelector for its own — so it waits for `enabled`.
 */
function useRcSections(enabled: boolean) {
  const [sections, setSections] = React.useState<RcSection[] | null>(null);
  const [error, setError] = React.useState<string | null>(null);

  React.useEffect(() => {
    if (!enabled) return;
    let cancelled = false;
    loadAllIndexPassages()
      .then((passages) => {
        if (!cancelled) setSections(buildRcSections(passages));
      })
      .catch(() => {
        if (!cancelled) setError("Reading Comprehension sections could not be loaded.");
      });
    return () => {
      cancelled = true;
    };
  }, [enabled]);

  return { sections, error };
}

/**
 * A status line that assistive tech is actually told about.
 *
 * Every wait on this page used to be a plain paragraph that appeared and
 * disappeared in silence: a screen reader user pressed a door and heard
 * nothing until they went looking.
 */
function Working({ children }: { children: React.ReactNode }) {
  return (
    <div
      className="flex items-center gap-3 py-4 text-sm"
      role="status"
      aria-live="polite"
    >
      <Loader2 className="h-4 w-4 animate-spin" aria-hidden />
      {children}
    </div>
  );
}

/**
 * Picking one of 59 PrepTests.
 *
 * A plain select made this a 59-row scroll starting at the oldest test, with no
=== 340,395 ===
  );
}

export default function TestingCentre() {
  const navigate = useNavigate();
  const { manifest, isLoading, error } = useQuestionBank();
  const [door, setDoor] = React.useState<Door>(null);
  const { sections: rcSections, error: rcError } = useRcSections(door === "test");
  const [runs, setRuns] = React.useState<PracticeTestRun[]>([]);

  React.useEffect(() => {
    setRuns(listPracticeTestRuns().filter((r) => r.status === "in-progress"));
  }, []);

  const tests = React.useMemo(
    () => (manifest && rcSections ? buildPracticeTests(manifest, rcSections) : []),
    [manifest, rcSections],
  );

  const beginTest = (blueprint: PracticeTestBlueprint, pace: PracticeTestPaceId) => {
    const run = createPracticeTestRun(blueprint, pace);
    navigate(practiceTestPath(run.id));
  };

  const discard = (run: PracticeTestRun) => {
    deletePracticeTestRun(run.id);
    setRuns((current) => current.filter((r) => r.id !== run.id));
  };

  // A door's panel renders below the two door buttons, which on a laptop puts it
  // under the fold: the click looks like it did nothing, and the section type
  // picker (Logical Reasoning / Reading Comprehension) is never seen, so the door
  // reads as LR-only. Bring the panel to the student when it opens.
  const panelRef = React.useRef<HTMLDivElement | null>(null);
  React.useEffect(() => {
    if (!door) return;
    const prefersReducedMotion = window.matchMedia?.(
      "(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)",
    ).matches;
    panelRef.current?.scrollIntoView({
      behavior: prefersReducedMotion ? "auto" : "smooth",
      block: "start",
    });
  }, [door]);

  return (
    <>
      <h1 className="classroom-title">Testing Centre</h1>
      <p className="testing-lede">
        Official LSAT material, timed like test day.
      </p>

      <ResumeList
        runs={runs}
        onResume={(run) => navigate(practiceTestPath(run.id))}
        onDiscard={discard}

## assistant

# `$impeccable audit` — Testing Centre

Scope: `/testing` (lobby, both doors, both panels, all five controls and their portals) at 1134px and 375px, light theme. Every number below was measured live in the browser, not read off the source.

## Audit Health Score

| # | Dimension | Score | Key finding |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Accessibility | 3 | Timing trigger declares `aria-haspopup="dialog"` over a `role="listbox"` popup |
| 2 | Performance | 3 | 59 and 54 option rows rendered eagerly, no virtualisation |
| 3 | Theming | 3 | Timing popover borrows the drill hub's token block, not the Testing Centre's |
| 4 | Responsive | 3 | Dropdown option rows are 32px against a 44px floor |
| 5 | Anti-patterns | 4 | No tells found |
| **Total** | | **16/20** | **Good** |

Previous run: 10/20. The dimension that moved most is Accessibility, from 1 to 3.

## Anti-patterns verdict: pass

Nothing in `testing-centre.css` or the three components matches the ban list. Grepped and confirmed zero: gradients, `background-clip: text`, `backdrop-filter`, decorative blur, `shadow-lg/xl/2xl`, `hover:scale-*`, bounce easing, `rounded-2xl/3xl`. Zero hardcoded colours outside comments; 29 `var(--classroom-*)` references carry the whole surface.

The closest thing to a tell is the two doors: same-sized cards, heading plus body plus meta. It survives because there are exactly two of them and they are the only two things a student can do in this room. It is a fork, not a grid.

The score report is a 56px figure with a small label, which is adjacent to the hero-metric template. It escapes because there are no supporting stat tiles and no accent gradient, and because an LSAT score genuinely is one number.

## Executive summary

- **16/20** (Good)
- **0 P0 · 1 P1 · 5 P2 · 2 P3**
- Top three: the two doors disagree about how you pick a PrepTest; the timing control's ARIA contradicts itself; dropdown rows are two-thirds of the minimum touch target.

## Findings

### [P1] The two doors use different PrepTest pickers

**Location** `src/components/drill/SectionSelector.tsx:425` vs `PrepTestPicker` in `src/pages/TestingCentre.tsx`
**Category** Accessibility / UX consistency

Measured, both doors open, same page:

| | Section door | Practice test door |
|---|---|---|
| Control | Radix `Select` | Popover + cmdk |
| Search input | **0** | 1 (`Search by number`) |
| Options | 54 | 59 |
| To reach PT 131 | scroll | 3 keystrokes |

This is my own miss from the execution round. I replaced the picker on the practice-test door and left the section door on the raw `Select`. A student who opens both doors in one session meets two different interaction models for the identical decision.

**Recommendation** Lift `PrepTestPicker` out of `TestingCentre.tsx` and use it for `preptest-select` in `SectionSelector`. The search filter, the newest-first ordering, and the `aria-labelledby="X-label X"` naming come with it.
**Suggested command** `$impeccable polish`

### [P2] Timing trigger's `aria-haspopup` contradicts its popup

**Location** `src/components/drill/SectionTimingPicker.tsx:243`
**WCAG** 4.1.2 Name, Role, Value (A)

The trigger sets `aria-haspopup="dialog"`. The element it opens is `role="listbox"` with four `role="option"` children carrying correct `aria-selected`. A screen reader announces a dialog and then lands in a listbox.

**Recommendation** `aria-haspopup="listbox"`, or promote the trigger to `role="combobox"` to match the other four controls in the form.

### [P2] Timing trigger's name drops its own value

**Location** `SectionTimingPicker.tsx:243`
**WCAG** 4.1.2

Visible text is `TIMING / Standard / 35:00`. The accessible name is `Section timing`, because `aria-label` overrides the subtree. The four sibling comboboxes all use `aria-labelledby="X-label X"` and announce their value. This one does not, so a screen reader user tabbing the form hears the current setting for Status, PrepTest, Section and Section type, then silence for Timing.

**Recommendation** Drop the `aria-label` and use the same self-referencing `aria-labelledby` as the rest of the form.

### [P2] Search results are never announced

**Location** `PrepTestPicker`, `src/pages/TestingCentre.tsx`
**WCAG** 4.1.3 Status Messages (AA)

Typing `131` filters 59 options to 1. Verified: the DOM's only polite live region is empty, so nothing is announced. A sighted user sees the list collapse; a screen reader user gets no confirmation the query matched anything.

**Recommendation** A visually hidden `role="status"` reading `1 PrepTest` / `No PrepTests match "…"`.

### [P2] Dropdown option rows are 32px

**Location** Both PrepTest pickers, and the Status and Section selects
**WCAG** 2.5.5 Target Size

Measured 32px tall against a 44px floor, across 59 rows in one picker and 54 in the other. This is Radix's `SelectItem` default. Every other control on the surface is exactly 44px, so this is the one place the rule breaks.

**Recommendation** Raise `SelectItem` / `CommandItem` padding to `min-h-[44px]` for this surface.

### [P2] "Adjust in settings" link is 101.6 × 15.2px

**Location** `src/components/drill/SectionTimingPicker.tsx:400`
**WCAG** 2.5.5

An inline `Link` inside the timing popover, well under 44px in both axes and the only navigation out of the flow at that point.

### [P3] `aria-controls` dangles while the popover is closed

**Location** `SectionTimingPicker.tsx`

The trigger carries `aria-controls="radix-:r7:"`. Verified: `document.getElementById('radix-:r7:')` returns null while closed, because Radix unmounts the content. An IDREF that resolves to nothing is invalid ARIA. Low impact and Radix-default, which is why it is P3 rather than P2. The doors do this correctly: `aria-controls="testing-door-panel"` resolves whether a panel is open or not.

### [P3] The timing popover wears the drill hub's theme class

**Location** `SectionSelector.tsx:372`, `:432`, and the timing popover content
**Category** Theming

The portalled content carries `drill-observatory-launcher-panel`, the drill hub's token block, rather than the `.testing-menu` block written for the Testing Centre's portals. It renders correctly in light, but it means a control inside the Testing Centre is painted from the observatory's palette. Two token layers now govern one room.

## Systemic patterns

Both real patterns trace to the same root: **shadcn primitive defaults surface wherever the local overrides didn't reach.** The 32px rows, the dangling `aria-controls`, and the `haspopup` mismatch are all Radix out-of-the-box behaviour. The surface's own CSS is disciplined; the gaps are all at the boundary where a Radix component was dropped in without a local override.

The second pattern is **`SectionSelector.tsx` is a shared component being audited as a Testing Centre surface.** It is the drill hub's component that the section door reuses, which is why it carries the observatory's theme class and its own picker. Its only production consumer is `TestingCentre.tsx`, so the divergence is fixable without touching the drill hub, but the file's ancestry explains every P1/P3 above.

## What is working

Genuinely clean, measured rather than assumed:

- **Zero contrast failures.** Lobby, both panels, and both portals, at 1134px and 375px. Every text node meets 4.5:1 (or 3:1 where large).
- **Every control is named.** Five comboboxes and every button return a non-empty accessible name. The `<label for>`-on-a-`<button>` trap that left all five anonymous last round is closed.
- **Focus is visible on real keyboard input.** Tab to the first door gives `:focus-visible` true, 1.6px solid `rgb(18,18,18)` at 1.6px offset. Tested with an actual Tab keypress, not a programmatic `.focus()`.
- **No overflow at 375px.** `scrollWidth === clientWidth === 375` in the lobby and with either panel open.
- **Disclosure ARIA is correct.** `aria-expanded` flips on the right door only, `aria-controls` resolves to a real element in every state.
- **Loading states are announced.** `role="status" aria-live="polite"` on the waits.
- **RC index loads lazily**, gated on `door === "test"`, instead of on mount.
- **Reduced motion is respected** on the panel scroll.
- **13 tests pass, typecheck clean.**

## Not verified this round

**Dark mode.** You asked me to drop it mid-run, so I did. Flagging it because last round's single worst finding (shadcn controls at 1.01:1, white on white) existed only in dark, and the P3 above means the timing popover is themed by a different token block than the one I fixed. The Theming score of 3 is a light-only score.

2. **[P2] `$impeccable harden`** — the ARIA set on `SectionTimingPicker`: `haspopup` to match the listbox, name to include the value, drop the dangling `aria-controls`.
3. **[P2] `$impeccable adapt`** — 44px minimum on `SelectItem` / `CommandItem` rows and the settings link.
4. **[P2] `$impeccable clarify`** — a status message for search results.
5. **[P3] `$impeccable polish`** — swap `drill-observatory-launcher-panel` for `.testing-menu` on the Testing Centre's portals.

You can ask me to run these one at a time, all at once, or in any order you prefer.

Re-run `$impeccable audit` after fixes to see your score improve.

/**
 * Picking one of 59 PrepTests.
 *
 * A plain select made this a 59-row scroll starting at the oldest test, with no
 * way to say "117" and be done. Newest first, because a student reaching for a
 * practice test reaches for a recent one, and because the section door next
 * door already orders them that way.
 */
function PrepTestPicker({
  tests,
  value,
  onChange,
}: {
  tests: PracticeTestBlueprint[];
  value: PracticeTestBlueprint | null;
  onChange: (test: PracticeTestBlueprint) => void;
}) {
  const [open, setOpen] = React.useState(false);

  return (
    <Popover open={open} onOpenChange={setOpen}>
      <PopoverTrigger asChild>
        <Button
          id="testing-pt"
          variant="outline"
          role="combobox"
          aria-expanded={open}
          aria-labelledby="testing-pt-label testing-pt"
          className="w-full justify-between font-normal"
        >
          {value ? `PrepTest ${value.pt} · ${value.questionCount} questions` : "Choose a PrepTest"}
          <ChevronsUpDown className="ml-2 h-4 w-4 shrink-0 opacity-50" aria-hidden />
        </Button>
      </PopoverTrigger>
      <PopoverContent
        align="start"
        className="testing-menu w-[--radix-popover-trigger-width] p-0"
      >
        {/* Substring, not cmdk's default fuzzy score. A PrepTest number is an
            identifier, and fuzzy matching keeps "PrepTest 130" on screen for a
            student who typed 131. Either the number is in the label or it is
            not. */}
        <Command
          filter={(value, search) => {
            const query = search.trim().toLowerCase();
            if (!query) return 1;
            return value.toLowerCase().includes(query) ? 1 : 0;
          }}
        >
          <CommandInput placeholder="Search by number" />
          <CommandList>
            <CommandEmpty>No PrepTest by that number can be sat end to end.</CommandEmpty>
            {tests.map((t) => (
              <CommandItem
                key={t.pt}
                value={`PrepTest ${t.pt}`}
                onSelect={() => {
                  onChange(t);
                  setOpen(false);
                }}
              >
                <Check
                  className={
                    value?.pt === t.pt ? "mr-2 h-4 w-4 opacity-100" : "mr-2 h-4 w-4 opacity-0"
                  }
                  aria-hidden
                />
                PrepTest {t.pt} · {t.questionCount} questions
              </CommandItem>
            ))}
          </CommandList>
        </Command>
      </PopoverContent>
    </Popover>
  );
}

}: {

  const chosen = ordered.find((t) => t.pt === pt) ?? null;
  const clock = formatPracticeTestClock(pace);

  if (tests.length === 0) {

[external_agent_tool_call: Bash]
description: Fix the timing trigger ARIA
command: python - <<'PYEOF'
import io
P='src/components/drill/SectionTimingPicker.tsx'
s=io.open(P,encoding='utf-8',newline='').read()

old = '''        <button
          type="button"
          aria-label="Section timing"
          className={cn(
            'w-full flex items-center justify-between gap-3 rounded-xl border border-border bg-background px-4 py-3 text-left transition-colors hover:bg-accent/40',
            'focus-visible:outline-none focus-visible:ring-2 focus-visible:ring-foreground/20',
            className,
          )}
        >
          <span className="min-w-0">
            <span className="block text-xs font-medium uppercase tracking-wider text-muted-foreground">
              Timing
            </span>'''

new = '''        <button
          type="button"
          /* Three corrections, all from the same audit finding.
             `aria-haspopup` said "dialog" while the thing that opens is a
             listbox, so a screen reader announced a dialog and then landed in
             a list of options. Radix defaults the attribute for a Popover; the
             popup here is a listbox, so it has to be said explicitly.
             `role="combobox"` matches the four sibling controls in this form.
             And the name is built from the visible text rather than an
             aria-label, so it carries the current value: every other control
             in the panel announces its setting and this one announced only its
             field. The ids are self-referencing for the same reason. */
          role="combobox"
          aria-haspopup="listbox"
          aria-expanded={open}
          aria-labelledby={`${fieldLabelId} ${valueLabelId}`}
          className={cn(
            'w-full flex items-center justify-between gap-3 rounded-xl border border-border bg-background px-4 py-3 text-left transition-colors hover:bg-accent/40',
            'focus-visible:outline-none focus-visible:ring-2 focus-visible:ring-foreground/20',
            className,
          )}
        >
          <span className="min-w-0">
            <span
              className="block text-xs font-medium uppercase tracking-wider text-muted-foreground"
              id={fieldLabelId}
            >
              Timing
            </span>'''
assert s.count(old)==1, 'trigger anchor drift'
s=s.replace(old,new,1)

old2 = '''            <span className="mt-0.5 flex flex-wrap items-baseline gap-x-2">
              <span className="text-sm font-semibold text-foreground">{triggerTitle}</span>
              <span className="text-xs text-muted-foreground">{triggerDetail}</span>
            </span>'''
new2 = '''            <span className="mt-0.5 flex flex-wrap items-baseline gap-x-2" id={valueLabelId}>
              <span className="text-sm font-semibold text-foreground">{triggerTitle}</span>
              <span className="text-xs text-muted-foreground">{triggerDetail}</span>
            </span>'''
assert s.count(old2)==1, 'value span anchor drift'
s=s.replace(old2,new2,1)

old3 = '''  const triggerTitle = isTimed ? labelFor(currentPercent) : 'Unlimited';'''
new3 = '''  // Stable per instance, so two of these on one page cannot collide.
  const reactId = React.useId();
  const fieldLabelId = `${reactId}-timing-label`;
  const valueLabelId = `${reactId}-timing-value`;

  const triggerTitle = isTimed ? labelFor(currentPercent) : 'Unlimited';'''
assert s.count(old3)==1
s=s.replace(old3,new3,1)

io.open(P,'w',encoding='utf-8',newline='').write(s)
print('SectionTimingPicker: role/haspopup/name corrected')
PYEOF
head -20 src/components/drill/SectionTimingPicker.tsx | grep -n "import"

description: Update SectionSelector tests
command: python - <<'PYEOF'
import io
P='src/components/drill/SectionSelector.test.tsx'
raw=io.open(P,encoding='utf-8',newline='').read()
crlf='\r\n' in raw
s=raw.replace('\r\n','\n')

old_helpers = """const openSelectShowing = async (text: RegExp) => {
  const trigger = (await screen.findByText(text)).closest('[role="combobox"]') as HTMLElement;
  trigger.focus();
  fireEvent.keyDown(trigger, { key: 'Enter', code: 'Enter' });
};

const pickSection = async () => {
  await openSelectShowing(/Select a PrepTest/);
  fireEvent.click(await screen.findByText(/^PrepTest 130$/));
  await openSelectShowing(/Select a section/);
  fireEvent.click(await screen.findByText(/^Section 3$/));
};"""

new_helpers = """const openSelectShowing = async (text: RegExp) => {
  const trigger = (await screen.findByText(text)).closest('[role="combobox"]') as HTMLElement;
  trigger.focus();
  fireEvent.keyDown(trigger, { key: 'Enter', code: 'Enter' });
};

/**
 * The PrepTest field is a Popover, not a Select, so it opens on click rather
 * than on the Select keyboard path above. Both doors of the Testing Centre now
 * share this one control; it used to be a 54-row scroll here and a searchable
 * combobox on the other door, for the same decision.
 */
const openPrepTests = async () => {
  fireEvent.click(await screen.findByRole('combobox', { name: /PrepTest/ }));
};

const searchPrepTests = async (query: string) => {
  fireEvent.change(await screen.findByPlaceholderText(/Search by number/), {
    target: { value: query },
  });
};

const pickSection = async () => {
  await openPrepTests();
  fireEvent.click(await screen.findByText(/^PrepTest 130$/));
  await openSelectShowing(/Select a section/);
  fireEvent.click(await screen.findByText(/^Section 3$/));
};"""
assert s.count(old_helpers)==1, 'helpers anchor'
s=s.replace(old_helpers,new_helpers,1)

old_order = """    renderSelector();
    await openSelectShowing(/Select a PrepTest/);

    await screen.findByText(/^PrepTest 131$/);"""
new_order = """    renderSelector();
    await openPrepTests();

    await screen.findByText(/^PrepTest 131$/);"""
assert s.count(old_order)==1, 'order anchor'
s=s.replace(old_order,new_order,1)

old_timing = "fireEvent.click(screen.getByRole('button', { name: /Section timing/i }));"
new_timing = """// Named by its visible text now, so the name carries the current value
    // rather than only the field. It was aria-label="Section timing", which
    // announced the field and dropped "Standard / 35:00".
    fireEvent.click(screen.getByRole('combobox', { name: /Timing/i }));"""
assert s.count(old_timing)==1, 'timing anchor'
s=s.replace(old_timing,new_timing,1)

# Two new tests covering the behaviour this change introduces.
old_tail = """  it('does not promise a clock that Unlimited never starts', async () => {"""
new_tail = """  it('filters the PrepTests by substring, not by fuzzy score', async () => {
    renderSelector();
    await openPrepTests();
    await screen.findByText(/^PrepTest 131$/);

    // commandScore keeps "PrepTest 130" alive for the query 131, because the
    // characters are all present in order. A PrepTest number is an identifier.
    await searchPrepTests('131');
    await waitFor(() =>
      expect(screen.getAllByRole('option').map((el) => el.textContent)).toEqual([
        'PrepTest 131',
      ]),
    );
  });

  it('announces how many PrepTests a search left', async () => {
    renderSelector();
    await openPrepTests();
    await screen.findByText(/^PrepTest 131$/);

    // Filtering is a status change with no other signal for a screen reader:
    // the list collapsing is a visual event only.
    await searchPrepTests('130');
    const status = await screen.findByRole('status');
    await waitFor(() => expect(status.textContent).toBe('1 PrepTest'));
  });

  it('says the timing trigger opens a listbox, because it does', async () => {
    renderSelector();
    await waitFor(() => expect(screen.queryByText(/Checking what you have worked/)).toBeNull());

    // It claimed aria-haspopup="dialog" while opening a role="listbox", so a
    // screen reader announced a dialog and then landed in a list of options.
    const trigger = screen.getByRole('combobox', { name: /Timing/i });
    expect(trigger.getAttribute('aria-haspopup')).toBe('listbox');
    expect(trigger.getAttribute('aria-labelledby')).toBeTruthy();
    expect(trigger.textContent).toMatch(/Standard/);
  });

  it('does not promise a clock that Unlimited never starts', async () => {"""
assert s.count(old_tail)==1, 'tail anchor'
s=s.replace(old_tail,new_tail,1)

if crlf: s=s.replace('\n','\r\n')
io.open(P,'w',encoding='utf-8',newline='').write(s)
print('SectionSelector.test.tsx updated: 3 helpers rewritten, 3 tests added')
PYEOF

## assistant

[external_agent_tool_call: Bash]
description: Document the token fix
command: python - <<'PYEOF'
import io
P='src/pages/testing-centre.css'
s=io.open(P,encoding='utf-8',newline='').read()
old = """/* shadcn's --muted-foreground is #737373, which clears 4.5:1 on white but only
   measures 4.24:1 on its own --muted fill — and that pairing is exactly the
   segmented control in the section launcher, where the unpicked half is a
   choice a student has to be able to read. Same hue, darkened to the
   classroom's text-safe meta: 4.80:1 on the fill, 5.37:1 on the panel. */"""
new = """/* shadcn's --muted-foreground is #737373, which clears 4.5:1 on white but only
   measures 4.24:1 on its own --muted fill — and that pairing is exactly the
   segmented control in the section launcher, where the unpicked half is a
   choice a student has to be able to read. Same hue, darkened to the
   classroom's text-safe meta: 4.80:1 on the fill, 5.37:1 on the panel.

   .testing-menu is in this rule for the same reason, one layer out. Three of
   this room's dropdowns used to carry the drill hub's token block, which is
   not media-gated: they opened as near-black panels on the white page. Moving
   them onto the classroom's own tokens fixed that and exposed this — the
   selected row of the timing menu fills to #ebebeb, and #737373 on it is
   3.98:1. The same darkening puts it back over the line. */"""
assert s.count(old)==1
s=s.replace(old,new,1)
io.open(P,'w',encoding='utf-8',newline='').write(s)
print('comment updated')
PYEOF
[/external_agent_tool_call]

## assistant

[external_agent_tool_call: Bash]
description: Give the menu text real contrast margin
command: python - <<'PYEOF'
import io
P='src/pages/testing-centre.css'
s=io.open(P,encoding='utf-8',newline='').read()
old = """   .testing-menu is in this rule for the same reason, one layer out. Three of
   this room's dropdowns used to carry the drill hub's token block, which is
   not media-gated: they opened as near-black panels on the white page. Moving
   them onto the classroom's own tokens fixed that and exposed this — the
   selected row of the timing menu fills to #ebebeb, and #737373 on it is
   3.98:1. The same darkening puts it back over the line. */
.classroom-root .testing-panel,
.testing-menu {
  --muted-foreground: 30 2.8% 41.6%;
}"""
new = """   A menu needs one step further. Three of this room's dropdowns used to carry
   the drill hub's token block, which is not media-gated: they opened as
   near-black panels on the white page. Moving them onto the classroom's own
   tokens fixed that and exposed the same pairing one layer out — the selected
   row of a menu fills to #ebebeb, darker than the panel, and #737373 on it is
   3.98:1. 41.6% would clear the bar at 4.51:1, which is a pass with nothing
   left over; 39% is 5.00:1, and a 12px clock reading is not the place to spend
   the last hundredth. */
.classroom-root .testing-panel {
  --muted-foreground: 30 2.8% 41.6%;
}

.testing-menu {
  --muted-foreground: 30 2.8% 39%;
}"""
assert s.count(old)==1
s=s.replace(old,new,1)
io.open(P,'w',encoding='utf-8',newline='').write(s)
print('menu muted-foreground -> 39% (5.00:1)')
PYEOF
[/external_agent_tool_call]

description: Commit the Testing Centre work
command: git commit -q -F - <<'EOF'
Testing Centre: one PrepTest picker, and menus that belong to this room

Five findings from the 16/20 audit, plus two the fixes exposed.

The two doors disagreed about how you pick a PrepTest. The practice-test door
had a searchable combobox; the section door had a plain Select, so the same
decision was 54 rows of scroll on one side of the room and three keystrokes on
the other. Both now use src/components/testing/PrepTestCombobox.tsx: substring
filter rather than cmdk's fuzzy score, because a PrepTest number is an
identifier and commandScore keeps 130 alive for someone who typed 131.

Filtering now announces its result. It was a status change with no signal for
anyone not watching the list collapse: 59 rows became 1 and a screen reader
said nothing. Debounced, so a three-digit number does not queue five
announcements.

The timing control said aria-haspopup="dialog" over a role="listbox", which is
Radix's Popover default and not what opens. It also carried an aria-label, so
its name was "Section timing" while the four comboboxes beside it announced
their values; it now takes its name from its visible text and reads
"Timing Standard 35:00". Same haspopup correction on the shared combobox.

Two things the audit got wrong, corrected here rather than left standing.

The 32px dropdown rows were called a target-size failure. ui/select.tsx already
carries min-h-[44px] sm:min-h-0 -- 44px where the finger is the pointer, 32px
where the mouse is -- and 32x440 clears WCAG 2.5.8 AA, which asks 24x24, not 44.
The measurement was desktop-only. CommandItem had no such rule, so it gets the
same pair; the SelectItem overrides were inert and are gone.

The observatory token block was called a tidiness problem. It is not media
gated, so three dropdowns in this room were opening near-black on the white
page. Moving them to .testing-menu fixed that and exposed the pairing one layer
out: #737373 on a selected row's #ebebeb fill is 3.98:1, so the menu's
--muted-foreground is darkened to 5.00:1.

Verified at 375 and 1280 in light: every panel and every open menu returns zero
contrast failures, zero unnamed controls, zero targets under 24px, no
horizontal overflow. 16 tests, typecheck clean.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
EOF

## assistant

All five done, pushed as `d0e23d5`. Viewport emulation cleared this time.

## What changed

**1. One PrepTest picker for both doors** (P1). New `src/components/testing/PrepTestCombobox.tsx`, used by `TestingCentre.tsx` and `SectionSelector.tsx`. The section door was 54 rows of scroll; it now searches. Measured: typing `131` → 1 row, `14` → 11 rows. Substring match, not cmdk's fuzzy score, so `131` no longer keeps `PrepTest 130` on screen.

**2. Search announces its result** (P2, WCAG 4.1.3). `role="status"` returning `"11 PrepTests"` / `"1 PrepTest"`, debounced 350ms so a three-digit number doesn't queue five announcements.

**3. Timing control's ARIA** (P2 ×2, WCAG 4.1.2). `aria-haspopup` was `"dialog"` over a `role="listbox"`; now `"listbox"`, with `role="combobox"` to match its four siblings. Its name was `"Section timing"` from an `aria-label` that hid the value; it now reads `"Timing Standard 35:00"`. Same `haspopup` correction applied to the shared combobox, where Radix's Popover default had the same problem.

**4. Menus rethemed** (P3 → actually P2). Covered below.

**5. Target size.** Covered below.

## Two things the audit got wrong

I'd rather flag these than let them stand:

**The observatory token block was worse than P3.** I graded it from the class name. `.drill-observatory-launcher-panel` is not media-gated, so in **light** mode three dropdowns in this room were opening near-black (`rgb(22,22,24)`) on a white panel. My contrast probe passed them because dark-on-dark is internally consistent. Screenshot confirmed it before the fix. That was a visible defect, not a tidiness note.

**The 32px rows were overstated.** `ui/select.tsx:108` already carries `min-h-[44px] sm:min-h-0` — 44px on touch widths, 32px on desktop pointer, deliberately. I measured desktop only. 32×440px clears WCAG 2.5.8 AA (24×24); it only misses the AAA 44px bar. I backed out the `SelectItem` overrides I'd added (they lost to the `sm:` rule anyway) and kept the fix only where it was real: `CommandItem` has no such rule, so it got the same `min-h-[44px] sm:min-h-0` pair. Verified 44px at 375, 30px at 1280.

**"Adjust in settings" (97×14px) needs no fix.** It's inline in a sentence, which is an explicit exception in both 2.5.5 and 2.5.8. I should have caught that when I filed it.

## A regression I made and fixed

Relighting the timing menu dropped its 12px detail text to **3.98:1** — it had been clearing 4.5 only because the dark panel it borrowed was doing the work. Same pattern as the `--muted-foreground` on `--muted` problem from last round, one layer out: `#737373` on a selected row's `#ebebeb` fill. Fixed at 5.00:1 rather than the 4.51:1 the panel's existing value would have given, because 4.51 is a pass with nothing left over.

## Verification

| | 375px | 1280px |
|---|---|---|
| Contrast failures | 0 | 0 |
| Unnamed controls | 0 | 0 |
| Targets under 24px | 0 | 0 |
| Horizontal overflow | none | none |
| Backdrop blurs | 0 | 0 |

Both panels and all five menus opened and swept individually at each width. 16 tests pass (3 new, covering substring filtering, the announcement, and the listbox declaration), typecheck clean, ESLint 0 errors on the touched files — the 2 warnings are pre-existing at lines 20 and 35, and my hunks start at 234.

Staged six files only. `classroom.css`, `ClassroomLayout.tsx`, and `ClassroomRouteViews.tsx` are the other session's work and are untouched in the tree.

**Dark mode is still unverified** — you took it off the table during the audit and I haven't put it back. The `.testing-menu` dark block now governs three more dropdowns than it did, so that's the one place I'd want eyes before calling this finished. Say the word and it's a two-minute check.

# Session Handoff — Testing Centre re-audit, then executing all five recommended fixes

## Where it started
Session resumed mid-thread. Joshua first asked why the Testing Centre work wasn't visible on main (it was — `1335713`), then asked for a second `$impeccable audit` of the Testing Centre, then told me to run all five recommended actions thoroughly. Two constraints emerged mid-session: **dark mode is out of scope** (he interrupted the audit to say so), and scope stays on the Testing Centre and nowhere else.

## Decisions locked + what shipped

Commit `d0e23d5` on `main`, six files, +317/−125.

- **One PrepTest picker for both doors** — the section door was a 54-row Select with no search while the practice-test door had a searchable combobox. New shared component at `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\projects\lsatu\src\components\testing\PrepTestCombobox.tsx`, consumed by both. Uses `shouldFilter={false}` with an explicit substring filter, because cmdk's `commandScore` keeps "PrepTest 130" alive for the query "131".
- **Search result count is announced** — `role="status"` + `aria-live="polite"`, debounced 350ms. WCAG 4.1.3. Lives in `PrepTestCombobox.tsx`.
- **Timing control ARIA corrected** — `aria-haspopup` was `"dialog"` over a `role="listbox"`; the `aria-label` also suppressed its value. Now `role="combobox"`, `aria-haspopup="listbox"`, name built from visible text via `React.useId()`-scoped `aria-labelledby`. In `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\projects\lsatu\src\components\drill\SectionTimingPicker.tsx` (hunks start at line 234).
- **Three dropdowns rethemed off `drill-observatory-launcher-panel`** — that class is **not** media-gated, so it painted near-black menus on the white light-mode page. Swapped to `.testing-menu` in `SectionSelector.tsx`.
- **Menu `--muted-foreground` darkened to `30 2.8% 39%`** (5.00:1) in `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\projects\lsatu\src\pages\testing-centre.css` — the retheme exposed `#737373` on a selected row's `#ebebeb` fill at 3.98:1. Held separate from `.testing-panel`'s `41.6%`.
- **Two audit findings corrected, not implemented** — the 32px row finding was overstated (`ui/select.tsx:108` already has `min-h-[44px] sm:min-h-0`; 32×440 passes 2.5.8 AA at 24×24). Inert `SelectItem` overrides backed out; `CommandItem` got the same pair since it has no such rule. The 97×14px "Adjust in settings" link is exempt under the inline-in-a-sentence exception of 2.5.5/2.5.8 and was left alone.
- **Audit score: 16/20** (was 10/20). 0 P0 / 1 P1 / 5 P2 / 2 P3. Scored light-only.

## Key files for next session
